| `stat_correction_window` | Thu 06:00 ET | 0h–7d | v2.0. How long after the week official stat corrections auto-apply (§23.4). Incumbent norm: Sleeper through Thursday; Yahoo until the next week's first game. Matchups show `final (pending corrections)` until it closes. |

#### 7.3.7 Tiebreakers (standings & playoff seeding)
Ordered, reorderable list, applied whenever two or more teams are tied after the primary sort (Win %) — this is the same chain standings display uses and playoff seeding uses at end of regular season, so there's exactly one ranking rule in the product.

**v1 default (Chris's ruling):** **(1) Win %, (2) Points For, (3) Head-to-head, (4) Points Against — higher wins, (5) Division record (if divisions) *(v2.16.12 — INERT for v1: divisions are pinned at 1 by the Q30 ruling, so this entry never separates a tie; it stays in the chain so the stored array shape is unchanged when divisions return — PROGRESS F221)*, (6) Coin flip (deterministic seeded).** Stored as an ordered array in `settings`; fully reorderable by the commissioner. (2)–(4) are the chain that actually matters for v1; (5)–(6) exist purely as a deterministic fallback for the near-impossible case all three tie exactly, and are candidates to trim later if you'd rather force a hard stop sooner.
- **Points Against direction is deliberate, not a typo:** the team that *allowed* more points played a harder schedule, so more Points Against ranks higher when Points For and head-to-head don't separate two teams.
- **Head-to-head only resolves a clean two-team tie** — the win-loss record between exactly those two teams' meetings that season. With three or more teams still tied after Points For, a round-robin comparison has no single well-defined winner (records can cycle: A beat B, B beat C, C beat A), so head-to-head is **skipped** for that group and the chain falls straight to Points Against (E63).
- **`total_points` leagues (§11.7) have no matchups, so no head-to-head data exists at all** — the chain skips directly from Points For to Points Against for those leagues (E64).
- *(v2.16.23 — migration 117 `league_standings(league_id)`, L.D1.7, measured — the chain as built; ~~no rule changed~~ **[R801: ONE NEW definition — Points Against = the primary opponent's points, once per week, the PF mirror — folded here where the spec was silent (116 computed no PA at all; 117:1069 is the first). Filed as PROGRESS §3 Q38 for Chris; stands PROVISIONALLY until ruled.]** The standings table is the league's FINAL `team_week_results` rows of the REGULAR SEASON only (`week < first week + regular_season_weeks`, NFL-week terms — D288; a playoff week never moves it — "at end of regular season"; provisional rows never count — §11.5). The record is the COMBINED one (§11.7 — h2h + median + second results; a `bye` is not a game); Win % = (2W + T) / 2G exact; Points For = Σ `points` (once per week, §11.7); **Points Against = Σ the PRIMARY opponent's points that week, once** — the mirror of PF, so Σ PF ≡ Σ PA over a league (a second-opponent game adds no PA, exactly as it adds no PF); a missing opponent row is counted and named in the payload, never a silent 0. The stored `tiebreakers` array is applied IN ITS STORED ORDER, `win_pct` included wherever it sits (the catalog default puts it first; entries a trimmed array omits are appended in the default order so the chain is always total; an unknown or duplicated entry refuses). Head-to-head for a CLEAN two-team tie = the two teams' meetings across primary AND `secondary` rows, wins minus losses (ties count for neither; equal ⇒ unresolved, the chain continues); a 3+ group skips the entry and is NAMED in the payload (E63); a `total_points` league skips it by `schedule_mode` — by name, never because no data happened to exist (E64; pinned in pgTAP 065 with rows that WOULD separate the pair). Entry (5) is inert (key 0). The coin flip is seeded on the league's stored `schedule_seed` (§11.7 — the only seed a league owns; the league id when none was ever minted, named in the payload), md5 → 60 bits, never `random()` — the same seed gives the same order every time; a Remix re-mints the seed and so may re-flip an EXACT tie (PROGRESS F246). Every row carries `separated_by` — the first entry whose key differs from the row above. PROGRESS D314; pgTAP 065.)*

#### 7.3.8 Draft configuration
Lives on the league `settings` and is hydrated into a `drafts` row when scheduled (§8/§12).

| Setting | D | R / Options | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `draft_type` | `snake` | **`snake`**, **`auction`**, `linear` | Linear is a cheap variant of snake; v1 QA focuses on snake + auction. |
| `snake_reversal` | false | true/false | 3rd-round reversal (Sleeper-style) when snake. |
| `draft_order_mode` | `random` | `random`, `manual`, `custom` | Manual = commissioner drag; custom = saved order. |
| `draft_order` | — | array of team_ids | Set/locked before start; commissioner editable. |
| `pick_timer_seconds` | 90 | **0 (untimed), 30, 45, 60, 90, 120, 180, 300, 600, 3600, 14400, 28800, 86400** | 0 = no clock; large values enable slow drafts (up to 24h). |
| `auction_budget` | **200** | 50–1000 | One-time draft budget. Separate from FAAB. |
| ~~`auction_min_bid`~~ | — | — | **REMOVED v2.13** (Chris, 2026-08-20 — *"we only need a min bid of $1 more right now"*). It named one thing and did three, and governed none of them honestly: it was the **nomination floor**, it was the **per-slot reserve** in the max-bid formula, and it was **not** the bid increment (raises have always been `> high_bid`, i.e. $1, at every value of this field — so a league setting "Minimum bid: $5" got a $5 nomination floor, $5 reserved per empty slot, and **$1 raises**). The two jobs it really did now follow `auction_zero_dollar_nominations`; the increment is fixed at $1 (§8.6.3). Leagues that stored a value are migrated when the field is dropped — a stored `0` becomes the toggle ON, anything ≥ 1 becomes the toggle OFF. |
| `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` | **false** | true/false | **NEW v2.13** (Chris, 2026-08-20 — *"a setting that allows $0 nominations"*). OFF (the default): a nomination must open at **≥ $1** and every team keeps **$1 in reserve per still-empty roster slot** (`max_bid = remaining − (open_slots − 1) × 1`). ON: a nomination may open at **any amount the nominator can afford, including $0**, and there is **no per-slot reserve** (`max_bid = remaining`) — *"with $0 nominations there is no $1 per slot reserve."* The **bid increment is $1 in both columns**; this toggle never touches it (*"you can't have a $0 minimum bid, those are two different settings"* · *"Nomination and Min Bid need to be different"*). See §8.6.1–8.6.3 for the table, and §8.6.8 for what stops binding when it is ON. |
| `auction_nomination_seconds` | 30 | 10–120 | Time to nominate. |
| `auction_bid_seconds` | 20 | 10–60 | Bid clock; **anti-snipe**: a bid with < `anti_snipe_threshold` left resets clock to it. **The opening window is the expensive number in an auction — see §8.6.3's pacing note; the default is under review (PROGRESS Q17).** |
| `auction_anti_snipe_seconds` | 10 | 0–15 | 0 disables anti-snipe. |
| `nomination_order_mode` | `same_as_draft_order` | `same_as_draft_order`, `random`, `manual` | Circular. `manual` reads `nomination_order` below. |
| `nomination_order` | — | array of team_ids | **NEW v2.13** — the auction's counterpart to `draft_order`, and for the same reason: `manual` needs somewhere pre-draft to store the order the commissioner set. Set/locked before start, commissioner editable, hydrated into `drafts.nomination_order` at start; edited post-start through the §8.7 order control. Chris, 2026-08-20: *"a modal window that pops up that lets the commissioner order the teams. And then a confirm new order button. We need this for snake drafts too, many leagues have rules of what the order should be based on last season standing."* **Snake's half already ships** (`draft_order_mode: manual`/`custom` + the drag-to-reorder editor in League settings); this row is what makes the auction's third mode reachable at all. |
| `autopick_default` | `queue_then_board_then_adp` | (fixed strategy) | Queue → primary league-tagged board → season Big Board → ADP. See §8.4. |
| `disconnect_grace_seconds` | 30 | 0–120 | Disconnected manager isn't auto-picked until grace elapses (ESPN-style). |
| `draft_scheduled_at` | — | timestamptz | When the room opens. |
| `time_zone` | null | valid IANA zone name (or null) | *(Erratum v2.9.2 — D98/C27, additive)* The league's named draft reference zone (§16.4). **Display-only metadata**: instants (`draft_scheduled_at`, `drafts.current_deadline`) stay the authority; when set, league-time renders in this zone (Intl), when null the stored-offset render stands. The draft-setup surface offers the scheduler's own zone as the one-tap default. |

**Validation rules (enforced in API + DB constraints):**
- `team_count ∈ {8,10,12,14,16}` (v1).
- `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1` — strict continuity (Q10 ruling, v2.8.6): the playoffs begin the week after the regular season ends; no overlap, no gap weeks. Applies regardless of `playoff_teams` — the field is effectively derived (§7.3.1), so a points-only league (playoff_teams 0) stores the consistent value too.
- `playoff_start_week + (playoff_rounds × playoff_weeks_per_round) − 1 ≤ 18`. *(v2.16.12 — this bound is checked in LEAGUE-week terms at creation and settings edit; the NFL-calendar fit of a MID-season league is the engine's job at `draft_start` (pre-flight) and `in_season` entry, per §11.7 **Mid-season entry** — Q29/Q31.)*
- Sum of starting slots ≥ 1 and ≤ 20; `roster_size` large enough that `roster_size × team_count` ≤ draftable player pool.
- Every starting slot has a unique `key` and a non-empty `eligible` set; **flex slots list ≥ 2 eligible positions**, single-position slots exactly 1; `bench` 0–20 and 0–6 IR spots.
- Each IR spot has a `type` (`unrestricted` | `restricted`) and ≥ 1 eligible designation; **restricted** spots set `min_weeks` ≥ 1 (default 4).
- A Swap assignment (when `swap_spots = 1`): the *out* player is a current starter, the *in* player is on the bench with the **same `player.position`**; **at most one swap per team per week**; editable only before the swap's lock.
- Auction: `auction_budget ≥ roster_size × reserve`, where `reserve` = **1** with `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` OFF and **0** with it ON (every team can fill a legal roster). *(v2.13 — this bullet used to read `× auction_min_bid`; the field is gone and the reserve is now derived from the toggle. With the toggle ON the bullet is satisfied by every legal budget, which is the point: at $0 nominations nothing can price a team out of a roster spot.)* *(v2.13.3 — **corrects v2.13.2, which got this wrong in the direction that matters.** With the reserve capped at $1 the bullet is REDUNDANT WITH ITS OWN SIBLING — the roster-size bullet above it (`sum of starting slots ≥ 1 and ≤ 20`, plus `bench` 0–20 and ≤ 6 IR) — so at reserve 1 nothing can violate solvency without violating that bullet too, and the arm never fires ALONE. **It is NOT unreachable, and v2.13.2's claim that it was is withdrawn:** the 20-slot cap is a VALIDATION RULE, not a schema bound, and the schema does not bound the `starting_slots` array at all, so a 30-slot lineup at `count` 10 is schema-valid and derives a 307-player roster against which a $50 budget fails this bullet per-field (measured, not reasoned: `leagueSettingsSchema.parse` returns OK and `validateLeagueSettings` returns both `roster_settings.starting_slots` and `draft.auction_budget`). Redundancy between two arms of one validator is not vacuity, and it is emphatically not permission to delete either — never-weaken. Pinned at the unit layer in `validate-league-settings.test.ts` (the arm firing on a Zod-parsed object) and at the API surface in `settings-round-trip-db.test.ts`; the engine's own start gate is a different and smaller count — D91 draftable slots, IR excluded — and is pinned one dollar either side in pgTAP 040 §D.)*
- Auction: `nomination_order` is required and must be a permutation of the active franchises **exactly when `nomination_order_mode = manual`** — the same rule `draft_order` obeys under `draft_order_mode: manual`/`custom`. Validated at draft start; a league that stores `manual` without a valid order is refused loudly, by name, with the way out in the message (v2.13; PROGRESS F80).
- Exactly one scoring system referenced and readable by the league; on draft start its rules are snapshotted (§7.3.3) — a league in `drafting`+ must always have a non-null `scoring_rules_snapshot`. *(v2.11: the referenced row is a template OR the league's own §7.3.3.1 forked custom row — nothing else; a format-2 rules doc must pass the §7.3.3.1 guardrail validation, and draft start refuses to snapshot an invalid doc — loud failure, never a silently-dropped key.)*
- Schedule (v2.0): generation invariants of §11.7 hold (each team exactly once per week per game type; both home- and away-side uniqueness; repeats separated ≥ 3 weeks; ~~divisions play 2× when weeks allow~~ *(v2.16.12 — struck for v1, Q30; F221)*); `second_opponent` derangement never pairs a team with its primary opponent.
- Locks (v2.0): all kickoff-derived locks (lineup, add/drop, swap, trade-defer) are evaluated from `nfl_games.kickoff_at` at runtime — precomputed lock timestamps are forbidden outside `league_player_pool.locked_until`, which the `lineup-lock` job refreshes on every run (§23.3).

### 7.4 Attaching ranking lists to a league (list ↔ league tie-in)
FieldScout's atomic unit is the ranking list (`docs/01-PRD.md` §F2). This ties a user's lists to the leagues they play in, so the rankings they built for *this* league are one tap away in the Live Draft Tool and all season.

- **Attach.** From any list (list detail, list card, or Big Board): **"Attach to league"** → pick from leagues the user belongs to. Reverse entry point from the league: **"Add a draft list."**
- **Many-to-many.** A list can be attached to multiple leagues; a league shows each member their own attached lists.
- **Primary draft board.** A member can mark one attached list as their **primary board** for the league — it feeds the draft queue and autopick (§8.4, §8.9).
- **Share (optional).** A member can mark an attachment **shared with the league** (e.g., the commissioner posts agreed keeper values); shared lists appear to all members, private ones stay visible only to their owner (RLS §12.15).
- **Non-destructive.** The list stays an independent object; detaching only removes the association — it never edits or deletes the list.
- **Smart suggestions.** When a list's scoring/format matches the league (e.g., a Superflex-PPR list ↔ a Superflex-PPR league), surface a one-tap "Attach to {league}." The league-create wizard also offers to attach existing lists.
- Framed in the UI with the familiar **tag** language, but stored as a first-class association (§12.15) — not a free-text tag — so the draft room, queue, and autopick can use it.

---

## 8. Feature B — Custom Draft Engine (the core)

The draft room is the highest-stakes, most concurrency-sensitive surface in the app. It must feel instant, never double-assign a player, never let a manager overspend, and let the commissioner fix anything live. The engine is **server-authoritative**: clients never decide who is on the clock, what time is left, or whether a pick is legal — the server does, and broadcasts truth.

### 8.1 Authoritative engine model
- **Single source of truth** is the `drafts` row (state, current pick index, deadline) plus the append-only `draft_picks` table. See §12.
- **Every state-changing action** (make pick, place bid, nominate, pause, undo, reassign, force pick) is performed by a **Postgres RPC function (`SECURITY DEFINER`)** called from a Route Handler, or by the Route Handler in a single transaction. The function:
  1. Locks the draft row (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE`) to serialize concurrent actions.
  2. Validates legality (turn, budget, exclusivity, slot availability, draft status).
  3. Writes the pick/bid/event.
  4. Advances state (next picker / next nomination) and sets the new server-side `current_deadline`.
  5. Returns the new authoritative state.
- **Clients subscribe** via Supabase Realtime (§9) to `drafts` (UPDATE) and `draft_picks` (INSERT/UPDATE) and re-render from the broadcast. The countdown is rendered locally from `current_deadline` (a server timestamp) — clients never own the clock.
- **Concurrency:** the row lock + a `UNIQUE` partial index guaranteeing a player can be picked once per draft (`UNIQUE(draft_id, player_id) WHERE NOT is_undone`) make double-picks impossible even under race conditions. The loser of a race gets a friendly "Player just went off the board" error.
- **Idempotency:** actions carry a client-generated `action_id`; replays (double-tap, reconnect retry) are no-ops.

### 8.2 Timer enforcement & autopick worker
- The pick/nomination/bid deadline is stored on `drafts.current_deadline`.
- A lightweight **`draft-tick` worker** (Supabase Edge Function invoked by pg_cron every ~5s while any `drafts.status = 'live'`, plus an immediate self-schedule on each action) checks for expired deadlines and performs the timeout action atomically (autopick, or in auction: assign to current high bid / advance nomination). This mirrors the existing two-speed cron pattern in `docs/02-TECHNICAL-ARCHITECTURE.md`. *(Erratum v2.8.13 — the vehicle (D87/C21, tasks-M2; migration 068): `draft-tick` ships as an in-database SQL RPC — `draft_tick()`, scheduled directly by pg_cron every 5s — no Edge Function, no HTTP hop; the repo has zero Edge Functions and its Vercel-cron floor is 60s. The "immediate self-schedule" is satisfied in-database: every action RPC sets the next deadline synchronously and the ≤5s cron enforces expiry (worst-case timeout-enforcement lag ~5s — the p95 pick→broadcast metric targets user actions, not timeouts); pg_net self-invocation is the recorded escape hatch if 5s proves coarse. The tick also owns the D94 auto-start scan: leagues in `scheduled` whose `draft_scheduled_at` has passed are created-if-absent and started each tick, so a league scheduled purely through the settings surface can never dead-end.)*
- Because enforcement is server-side, the draft is correct even if every client disconnects.
- **Soft timer option:** if `pick_timer_seconds = 0`, no auto-action; the clock is informational only (the commissioner advances things). Useful for casual/slow drafts. *(Erratum v2.10.3 — L.C1.2/migration 084: this is the SNAKE/LINEAR pick clock's option and does **not** reach an auction. `auction_nomination_seconds` (10–120) and `auction_bid_seconds` (10–60) are their own §7.3.8 fields with their own floors — there is no "0" among them — so an auction started with `pick_timer_seconds = 0` still opens with a nomination deadline and the tick still enforces both auction clocks. An untimed auction is not expressible in v1, deliberately: nomination and bid clocks are what make §8.6.7(b)'s no-raise award and §8.6.8's progress guarantee reachable at all.)*

### 8.3 Draft order
- **Snake/linear:** `draft_order` is an array of `team_id`s, set by `draft_order_mode` (random/manual/custom). Snake reverses each round; `snake_reversal` flips round 3. Linear repeats the same order every round.
- **Auction:** `nomination_order` is circular; `nomination_order_mode` controls it. *(v2.13 — `manual` is set **pre-draft, in League settings**, through the **same drag-to-reorder editor snake uses** for `draft_order`: one order-editing pattern in the product, two fields. `same_as_draft_order` stays the default and covers most leagues; `random` is seeded from the draft id so a random nomination order is not a carbon copy of a random draft order.)*
- Commissioner can edit order until the first pick (and after, as an override).
- A "Randomize Order" action with a visible animation (and an optional reveal screen) is supported; the result is written before the draft starts.

### 8.4 Queue & autopick
- Each manager has a personal **queue** (`draft_queues`): an ordered list of `player_id`s. Drag to reorder; players already drafted are auto-removed/skedaddled. *(v2.10 naming rule — Chris, 2026-08-15: the queue is surfaced in ALL user-facing UI as **"Targets"** — room tab, load-a-list actions, autopick copy ("Timeouts draft from the top of your Targets first"), empty states. Schema, API and internal names — `draft_queues`, the queue routes/RPCs — are unchanged; this is UI copy + component-label scope only.)*
- **Autopick strategy** (`queue_then_board_then_adp`): on timeout or when a manager is in auto mode, pick the highest available from (1) their queue, else (2) their **primary league-tagged draft board** if they set one (§7.4), else (3) their FieldScout season-long Big Board order, else (4) lowest `players.adp`. The pick must fit an open slot (respect roster needs: don't autopick a 3rd QB into a 1-QB league if other needs are open — "best available that fills a need"). **K/D-ST deferral (v2.0):** autopick treats K and D/ST as ineligible until the team's remaining picks equal its remaining required slots or the draft is within its final 3 rounds — no bot takes a kicker in round 3 (E30).
- Managers can toggle **"Auto-draft me"** (e.g., if they have to leave). Commissioner can toggle it for any team.

### 8.5 Snake draft flow
1. Room opens at `draft_scheduled_at` (or commissioner clicks **Start Draft**). Status → `drafting`.
2. Board shows: full pick grid (rounds × teams), available players (searchable/filterable, with FieldScout Big Board / tiers / ADP overlays), the on-the-clock team, the pick clock, my queue, my roster (slots filling up), and draft chat.
3. On the clock: the active manager selects a player → RPC validates (their turn, player available, fits roster *(Erratum v2.8.11: at manual pick time "fits roster" = capacity only — the advance math guarantees each team exactly `total_rounds` picks and rounds = draftable spots (D91); need-fit is §8.4 autopick's rule, and E16's bipartite slot-fit is M4's lineup validator — PROGRESS D105(7), tasks-M2 §1)*) → writes `draft_picks` row → advances to next picker, resets clock → Realtime broadcast updates everyone.
4. Timeout → autopick (§8.4).
5. Disconnect → after `disconnect_grace_seconds`, that team flips to autopick for the current pick; reconnect restores manual control. Presence (§9) shows who's online. *(Erratum v2.8.13 — the D102 mechanism (spec-absent schema; migration 068): connectedness is read from `draft_liveness(draft_id, user_id, last_seen_at, PK(draft_id, user_id))` — a heartbeat table written ONLY by the `draft_touch` RPC the room calls (~15s cadence + visibility change); deliberately its own table, never a `drafts` column, so heartbeats never contend with the draft-row lock; no client DML, no client SELECT, no broadcast (Presence stays the UI's who's-online source). A seat is FRESH while ≤ 2 beats are missed (45s — a pinned constant). At deadline expiry: `is_autodraft` seats, no-user seats (placeholder/vacated — E48), and FRESH seats autopick immediately (step 4); a STALE seat's pick is held open until deadline + `disconnect_grace_seconds` — a returning manager may pick manually during the hold (this step's "reconnect restores manual control"/E3) — then autopicks.)*
6. Repeat until every roster is full. Status → `in_season`; initial `league_rosters` are populated from `draft_picks`.

### 8.6 Auction draft flow
1. Room opens; each team shows remaining **budget** and **max bid** (`remaining − (open_slots − 1) × reserve`). *(v2.13 — `reserve` is **$1** by default and **$0** when `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` is ON, in which case `max_bid = remaining` flat. A complete roster reads `max_bid = 0` — E27, and it is what makes §8.6.9's uncontestable check free.)*
2. The nominating manager (per `nomination_order`) nominates a player at an opening bid (**≥ the nomination floor and ≤ their own max bid**); nomination clock enforces it (timeout → system nominates highest available or skips per setting). *(v2.13 — the **nomination floor** is **$1** by default and **$0** when `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` is ON: with the toggle ON *"a nomination should allow any number that the player can afford."* The floor is a nomination rule and nothing else — it does not govern raises; see step 3.)* *(Erratum v2.12.1 — the mechanism (tasks-M3 C32/D129(2); migration 086, task L.C1.4): **there is no skip setting and there never was** — §7.3.8's catalog has never carried one, so v1 ALWAYS system-nominates on timeout. The `draft-tick` worker runs the ON-CLOCK team's own §8.4 autopick resolution chain (queue → primary board → Big Board → ADP, with the documented greedy need-fit) at an opening bid of **the nomination floor** *(v2.13.2 — this clause read `auction_min_bid` until AP.1 retired that field; the floor is the derived number of §7.3.8's `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` row, $1 by default and $0 with the toggle on, and the system writer still opens exactly AT it)*, and writes that opening as a `draft_bids` row like any other so §12.5 carries one uniform history. **§8.4's K/D-ST deferral maps to its FORCED-only arm in an auction:** the printed "within its final 3 rounds" escape is the snake/linear pick clock's — an auction has no rounds (`drafts.current_round` there is a display-only rotation lap), so K and D/ST stay ineligible until a team's remaining buys equal its unfilled required seats. **The §8.5.5 disconnect contract carries to this clock unchanged:** `is_autodraft` seats, no-user seats (E48) and seats FRESH as of the deadline are system-nominated AT the deadline, while a STALE seat's nomination is held open until deadline + `disconnect_grace_seconds` — and a returning manager may still nominate manually during the hold, exactly as they may pick manually during the §8.5.5 hold.)*
3. Open bidding: any manager with sufficient max bid raises. Each bid resets the bid clock per **anti-snipe** (`auction_anti_snipe_seconds`). Bids are validated against live remaining budget and required roster slots.

   **The bid increment is $1, fixed, in every league (v2.13 — Chris, 2026-08-20).** A raise must exceed the standing high bid, and bids are whole dollars, so the minimum legal raise is always `high_bid + 1` — including from a $0 opening, which is raised to $1, never to $0. This has always been the engine's behaviour; what changes in v2.13 is that the settings catalog no longer carries a control that appeared to set it. **Correction of record:** `auction_min_bid` never governed raises. Any earlier statement — in this spec, in the settings UI, or in a refusal message — that read it as "the minimum bid" was describing the nomination floor and the per-slot reserve, not the increment. Every place the number still appears in bid-refusal copy is explaining the *max-bid formula's* reserve term (E5), not a raise minimum.

   **Pacing note — the opening window is the expensive number (v2.13).** `auction_bid_seconds` opens every nomination, and `auction_anti_snipe_seconds` (default 10) only re-floors a clock a bid lands inside. At the shipped 20s default that inverts the cost of the two cases: an **uncontested** player pays the full 20s of silence, while a **contested** one resolves on the 10s reset — the dull case costs double the exciting one. Over a default 12-team roster (16 draftable slots ⇒ 192 nominations, §7.3.2/D91 — *v2.16.10: recomputed for the Scout default roster's wr 3; was 15 ⇒ 180*) the opening windows alone are `192 × 20s = 64 minutes` of clock before a single keystroke or raise. Chris's targets, from real use on Sleeper at 12 teams: **a live draft with real managers under 90 minutes, a mock under 45** (§8.8). Two of the three levers are engine work (§8.6.9's instant award, §8.8's reactive CPU bidding); the third is this default, and it is **under review — PROGRESS Q17 carries the recommendation and awaits Chris's sign-off.** The catalog row above is unchanged until he rules.
4. Bid clock hits 0 → highest bidder wins; `draft_picks` row written with `price`; budgets recomputed; nomination advances.
5. Inactive/disconnected managers do not auto-bid by default (configurable later); they can still be nominated for and simply don't participate.
6. Repeat until all rosters legal/full or budgets exhausted (engine guarantees every team can always complete a legal roster via the max-bid formula).
7. **Endgame rules (v2.0):** (a) nomination validates the nominator can afford their own opening bid; (b) a bid clock expiring with no raises awards the player to the **nominator at the opening bid**; (c) teams whose rosters are complete are **skipped in the nomination rotation** and cannot bid; (d) when a team's max bid is $1, only $1 bids are accepted for it; (e) system nominations on timeout pick the highest-ADP player that fits *some* team's open slot, skipping players no team can legally roster. (E25–E27) *(Erratum v2.12.1, **reasoning corrected v2.12.3** — (b) vs (e) reconciled (tasks-M3 C33/D129(2); migration 086, task L.C1.4). **The engine behaviour is unchanged and was never in doubt: a system nomination runs the on-clock team's own §8.4 resolution chain.** ~~v2.12.1 argued that (e) as printed can select a player the NOMINATOR cannot roster, so that (b)'s no-raise award would be illegal.~~ **That reasoning is withdrawn (D163/R374).** Under Chris's 2026-08-18 ruling the draft never consults positional legality — **capacity is a pure count** — and (c)'s rotation-skip already guarantees the nominator has an open slot, so there is no player the nominator "cannot roster" and no legality contradiction to reconcile. **The correct reason the system nomination runs the nominator's own chain is a quality-of-outcome one: an absent manager gets a player their OWN board would have picked**, rather than an arbitrary player some other team happened to need — and, as a by-product, (e)'s "fits *some* team's open slot" is satisfied a fortiori. One implementation serves snake timeouts and auction nominations; no separate cross-league search exists, and none is owed.)*
8. **Solvency invariant (v2.0):** `remaining_budget ≥ open_slots × reserve` holds for every team at all times — including through commissioner budget edits and bid reversals, which are validated against it (a commissioner who needs more room reverses won bids instead; E28). *(v2.13 — `reserve` is the toggle-derived number of §8.6.1, and this is the one place the toggle's consequence must be stated plainly rather than left to be discovered. **With `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` ON the invariant becomes `remaining ≥ 0`: the machinery stays correct and stops binding.** The max-bid cap, E28's three arms, the insolvent-award refusal and the stuck-clock case are all still enforced, still re-checked at the award under the draft-row lock, and still pinned — they simply never fire, because every team can always fill every remaining slot at $0. That asymmetry is exactly why $0 nominations are a **toggle** and not a change of model: a league on the default keeps the guarantee the engine was built around — *"you can always finish your roster"* — and a league that opts in has traded it for a bidding style where nothing is unaffordable. Never weaken the invariant to accommodate the toggle; derive the reserve and let the algebra go slack on its own.)*

9. **Uncontestable nominations are awarded instantly (NEW v2.13 — Chris, 2026-08-20).** *"I would prefer a 3 second tick with the message. 'No one can bid. Awarding Player Name to the Nominator.' And yes it should apply no matter where it happens in the draft."*
   - **The condition, exactly:** at the instant a nomination opens, no **other** franchise can reach `high_bid + increment` — i.e. no active franchise besides the nominator has `max_bid ≥ high_bid + 1`. The check is written against `high_bid + increment` rather than `> high_bid` so it stays true if the increment ever stops being $1; today the two coincide.
   - **A complete roster is excluded for free** — §8.6.1 forces `max_bid = 0` when `open_slots ≤ 0`, so E27's skipped teams fail the test without a second clause. Under the $0 toggle a broke-but-open team is excluded too, for a different reason: taking a $0 nomination still costs $1, and it has nothing.
   - **It applies anywhere in the draft**, not only at the end. It is *most likely* in the last thirty-odd nominations, which is a fact about how auctions unfold and not a condition on the rule.
   - **The award happens in the nomination's own transaction** — the same award path a bid-clock expiry takes (same price, same `draft_picks` row, same rotation advance, same solvency re-check under the draft-row lock). It is not scheduled, not deferred to the tick, and it does not depend on a clock: a bid window nobody can enter is not a window. *(v2.13.4 — as built, AP.2/migration 093: the award is the **ordinary** award, extracted out of `draft_tick` ARM 2.6(b) and reused, so there is exactly one award implementation and no second kind of award. The uncontestable path adds only two things: an **additive `"uncontested": true` key** on the nomination's own phase-flip broadcast — the event every nomination already emits, so no new column, event or topic (§12.3's erratum below) — and a **NULL `current_deadline`** on that flip, which is what makes "no bid clock is ever opened" true on the wire and not only in the row. Both statements commit together, so the key **exists only on the wire and never at rest**: any SELECT already reads `current_nomination` NULL, which is precisely why a reconnect during the beat cannot lose the award, a late joiner sees the completed award, and the message cannot be shown twice.)*
   - **Every nomination path, and there are three (v2.13.4).** "A nomination" carries no qualifier about who opened one: the human `draft_nominate`, the §8.6.2 timeout / mock think-time `draft_system_nominate_internal`, **and the commissioner force-nomination that is `draft_force_pick`'s auction arm** (§8.7/R301). AP.2's breakdown listed the first two; the third was found at build time, the way AP.1 found the same function missing from its own read-site table (PROGRESS D214(1)).
   - **The 3 seconds is the room's presentation beat, not a server delay.** The nomination broadcast announces the award as uncontestable, every client renders *"No one can bid. Awarding {Player} to {Team}."* and holds it for 3 seconds. That makes the beat exact and identical for everyone, which a tick-scheduled 3s cannot be (the ≤5s sweep would land it anywhere in 3–8s, differently each time). **Open copy question — PROGRESS Q18:** whether the message names *"the Nominator"* (Chris's words) or the team (*"Awarding Bijan Robinson to Team Chris"*). *(**Erratum v2.13.4** — this bullet read "…and holds it for 3 seconds **before painting the board**", which had the order backwards. Chris, 2026-08-20, correcting a first reading of AP.2: **"No you've misunderstood. It awards the player immediately and then displays that message for 3 seconds until the next nomination."** So: the server finds the nomination uncontestable and **awards it immediately, atomically, in that same determination**; the message then displays for 3 seconds; then the next nomination opens. The beat lives in the gap that already exists **before the next nomination**, in the same family as the room's other nomination pacing — it is never a decision window, never a step on the award path, and there is no interval in which the award is pending. Nothing on the server waits (tasks-AP §4 rule 11).)*
   - **Nothing defends a window, because there is no window, and that is a ruling rather than an omission (v2.13.4).** The determination, the award and the commit are one transaction under the draft-row lock every nomination path already takes, so a commissioner budget edit, a void, a reconnect or a roster change runs entirely before the predicate (and is seen by it) or entirely after the award (and acts on a board where the player is bought). Chris, same session: *"but a commish can pause a draft and change anything or someone can disconnect at any time. TBH in drafts that's known as a 'personal problem'"* — **pause → change → resume (§8.7) is the sanctioned way to change a live draft, and a mid-auction disconnect is what §8.6.2's system nomination is for.** No pending state, no re-check-at-expiry, no cancellation path and no reconciliation path is owed here or anywhere else in this rule; a future session should read their absence as the ruling it is. What still stands, because it defends against two *writers* rather than a change of mind: the row lock, §8.1/E2 idempotency, and `uniq_draft_player_live`. If an award needs to come back, the remedy is unchanged and is `draft_undo`.
   - It composes with the $0 toggle rather than being weakened by it — see the second bullet. (E67)

### 8.7 Commissioner live draft controls (explicitly required)
All available from a **Draft Commissioner Panel** overlay in the room; **every one writes a `commissioner_actions` audit entry** (§10) and broadcasts.

> **v2.12 — where these controls live in the room (Chris, 2026-08-17).** The panel is reached from **one place and one place only: the `Draft Options` menu in the room's 54px command bar** (§16.4). **Draft Options absorbs the entire commissioner panel** — the timer edits *and* undo, cascade undo, reassign / move a pick, force pick, edit order, autopick toggles, seat controls, Manual Edit Mode, Reset, and End Draft. The separate floating "Commish panel" button that M2 shipped **is retired**; a second door to commissioner power is exactly the thing this consolidates away. **Pause and Resume are the two exceptions** — they are first-class buttons *on* the bar rather than menu items, because they are the controls a commissioner reaches for while something is going wrong and they must not be two clicks deep. Nothing about the controls' *semantics*, refusals, audit obligations, or system-chat posts changes here — this is where the door is, not what is behind it.

| Control | Behavior |
|---|---|
| **Pause / Resume** | Freezes/refreezes all clocks. Unlimited. Banner shows "Draft paused by {commish}". On pause the remaining time persists to `drafts.deadline_remaining_ms`; on resume `current_deadline = now() + remaining` — clocks never gain or lose time across pauses (v2.0). |
| **Edit pick clock** | Change `pick_timer_seconds` (applies to subsequent picks). *(v2.12.5 — F57 ALIGN: edited while paused, like every commissioner control; the extend-current-deadline clause is retired — a paused clock keeps its stored remaining time, and the pause itself is the time relief.)* |
| **Undo last pick** | Reverts the most recent pick (player returns to pool, clock rewinds to that team). |
| **Undo to a point (cascade)** | Undo back to pick #N (everything after is reverted). Clear confirm dialog showing what will be undone. |
| **Edit / reassign a pick** | Change which player a given pick selected, or **move a drafted player to a different team** (drag a player from Team A's roster to Team B in the panel). Validates exclusivity & slots; writes before/after to the log. *(This is the "move players between teams easily if something breaks" requirement.)* |
| **Pick for a manager** | Make the current pick on behalf of the team on the clock (disconnect/AFK). |
| **Toggle autopick for any team** | Force a team to/from auto mode. |
| **Adjust auction budget / undo a won bid** | Correct a mis-click: reverse a winning bid (player back to pool, budget restored) or adjust a team's remaining budget. |
| **Reassign a draft seat** | Swap which user controls a team (e.g., a friend takes over for a no-show). |
| **Reset draft** | Wipe all picks back to pre-draft (status → `scheduled`). Hard confirm; fully audited; cannot be silent. |
| **End draft** *(v2.10 — AUCTION; RULED v2.10.1, Chris 2026-08-16: end-as-is)* | Ends an auction early, as-is: drafted players keep their prices, **unfilled roster slots stay empty**, league → `in_season`; free agency fills the gaps later. Hard confirm listing the consequences; fully audited; system chat post; cannot be silent. Solvency is trivially preserved (ending spends nothing); the completion writer accepts partial rosters. (OQ 19, ruled — option (a).) |

*(v2.10 — auction commissioner UX, ruled by Chris 2026-08-15; applies to AUCTION drafts:)*
- **Pause-first is REQUIRED** for: undoing picks (single + cascade), Manual Edit Mode (reverse/reassign/move), editing the current nomination, and ALL timer edits. These controls refuse on a running auction with a friendly "pause the draft first" — this supersedes the controls-available-live posture above **for auction**. *(v2.12.5 — the snake half is RULED: F57 ALIGN, Chris 2026-08-18. Pause-first applies to EVERY draft type: `draft_undo` (both arms), reassign/move, and all clock edits refuse on a running snake/linear draft with the same one-gate refusal, and succeed paused. One mental model — the command bar's Pause button is the universal door to commissioner edits. Snake's extend-current arm is retired (see the Edit-pick-clock row); `draft_set_order`, force-pick, and reset keep their shipped postures — not part of the ruling. Migration 090 / task L.C1.8.)* *(v2.15 — **ONE carve-out, and it is mocks only: the clock edit is not pause-first on a MOCK** (§8.8's v2.15 block; Chris, 2026-08-20 — *"the user running the mock should be able to make it as fast as possible IMO and pause it if they need to do something"*). Pause-first exists to stop a clock moving under eleven other people mid-bid; solo it protects nobody and costs a pause every time the launcher wants to try a different number. **Nothing about a REAL draft changes** — every clause above stands exactly as ruled, at every draft type. The carve-out is `is_mock`, never `draft_type`, and it covers `draft_set_clock` only: the other five gated verbs (`draft_undo`, reassign/move, reverse-won-bid, cancel-nomination) edit draft state the tick is concurrently reading, so the pause is taking the tick out of the race as well as being a courtesy, and they stay pause-first in a mock too. The new value still governs the **next** clock and never rewrites a running one — the E15 rule, unchanged, and the extend-current arms stay retired on both draft types. Task lane `docs/specs/tasks-MS-mock-sandbox.md` (MS.3); PROGRESS D219.)*
- **Manual Edit Mode** *(replaces this table's "drag a player from Team A's roster to Team B" articulation for auction)*: the commissioner enters the mode, clicks any drafted player's cell → a modal with exactly two choices: **(a) "Reset pick"** — player returns to the available pool, the winning manager is refunded; **(b) "Move player to a different team"** — previous owner refunded, new owner charged $Amount, where the commissioner must **re-enter the cost** to confirm. Maps onto the reverse-won-bid and priced-reassign engine paths; validations (exclusivity, receiving-team solvency) unchanged.
- **Edit current nomination** *(while paused)*: cancel-and-renominate — the live nomination is voided (open bids voided, no award, the sequence number is NOT consumed), and on resume the same nominator renominates.
- Pause/Play, Reset, and every commissioner action remain **visible to all users** (system chat posts; §16.3).

**Disconnect/robustness requirements**
- Reconnect must restore the full room state in < 2s from the authoritative `drafts` + `draft_picks` snapshot (no reliance on missed broadcasts; client refetches state on reconnect, then resubscribes).
- If the commissioner disconnects, co-commissioners retain controls; if none, the draft auto-pauses after grace (configurable) so nothing runs unsupervised during an outage. *(Erratum v2.8.14 — the mechanism (D102/D108; migrations 068/069): the `draft-tick` outage arm reads commissioner/co-commissioner connectedness from `draft_liveness` (the §8.5.5 heartbeat table) and auto-pauses a live NON-mock draft when supervision was ESTABLISHED and then LOST — at least one commissioner/co-commissioner heartbeat row exists for the draft AND none is newer than now() − (the 45s freshness constant + `disconnect_grace_seconds`). Freshness here is evaluated AS OF NOW each tick — the deliberate contrast with the pick hold's as-of-deadline rule (R132): the outage check is a continuous present-tense supervision question with no fixed reference instant. A draft whose commissioners NEVER connected (zero heartbeat rows — e.g. a D94 auto-started room nobody opened) runs unsupervised BY DESIGN and is never outage-paused — this bullet's word is "disconnects", which presupposes a connection, and pausing never-attended drafts would defeat the D94 "absent rooms still draft correctly" contract (§8.5.4/E48 autopilot). The pause persists the remaining clock exactly like a manual pause (one bookkeeping implementation) and posts a system chat message; a returning commissioner ends the outage condition immediately but the draft NEVER auto-resumes — resume is a commissioner action.)*
- All ephemeral UI (who's typing, hover) uses Broadcast/Presence and is non-authoritative.

### 8.8 Draft chat & Mock Draft Mode (v2.3 — promoted from Phase-F nice-to-have to a core feature)
- **Draft chat:** real-time chat scoped to the draft (reuse `league_chat` with a `channel = 'draft:<draft_id>'`, or a dedicated `draft_chat`; spec uses `league_chat.context`). Commissioner override actions auto-post a system message into draft chat (non-disable-able) — Sleeper-style transparency.

**Mock Draft Mode — solo practice under the league's real settings.** One user drafts against CPU opponents with the actual countdown pressure; the product requirement is *timer fidelity*, not a toy.
- **Launch:** any league member starts a mock from a pre-draft league (`setup`/`scheduled`) — "Run mock draft." The mock snapshots the league's **real draft config** (type, pick clock, order incl. their actual slot, rounds from roster slots, anti-snipe) into a new `drafts` row with `is_mock = TRUE`. The human takes their real seat by default (any seat selectable); every other seat is a CPU. *(Erratum v2.16 — **THIS BULLET NOW DESCRIBES THE DEFERRED HALF OF MOCK DRAFT MODE, NOT THE SHIPPING ONE.** See the v2.16 block below: **a mock is STANDALONE** — it owns its settings, taken from the six base scoring templates and this spec's own defaults, and needs no league at all. **League-attached launch is DEFERRED, not cancelled** (Chris, 2026-08-21: *"we can get back to the league based mocks after we actually have demand for the league product itself"*), and everything in this bullet is what it returns as. Two consequences that are LAW now rather than when it returns: **(a)** the D103(1) full-seat-map requirement — which produced a live refusal in real use, *"practice draft failed, it says i need 11 more people"* — **does not apply to a standalone mock at all**, because there is no league seat map; a standalone mock mints its own bot opponents, and **a mock never refuses to start for want of humans**; and **(b)** the roster **shape** must be snapshotted like every other setting. **CLOSED by migration 094 (task MP.2) — erratum v2.16.1:** it was read **live** from `leagues.roster_settings->'starting_slots'` by `draft_autopick_resolve` and `draft_mock_cpu_need`, breaking D95's *"a mock never re-hydrates"* for the mocks that already existed. The launch snapshot now carries the WHOLE roster object at `drafts.config->'roster'`, both readers take the shape from the draft row on a mock's path, and **a REAL draft keeps the LIVE read, deliberately** (its settings edits go through §8.7/D141's pause-first commissioner controls; snapshot-at-start for real drafts is a separate question this erratum does not decide). Task lane `docs/specs/tasks-MP-mock-practice.md` (MP.2/MP.3); PROGRESS D226/D227/D236.)*
- **Same engine, literally:** server-authoritative deadlines, `draft-tick` timeouts, autopick, pause/resume, queue, board — the identical code path as a live draft. A mock that behaves differently from draft night is worse than no mock.
- **CPU opponents = the simulator bots** (delivery plan §4.2) exposed in-product — one bot implementation, two consumers. Rankings/ADP-driven with roster-need awareness; **humanized timing** (picks land at a random 20–70% of the clock, with occasional near-buzzer picks so the user *feels* timeout and anti-snipe behavior); auction bots bid value-based (ADP-derived values ± noise) and pass the same max-bid/solvency validator as humans.
- **CPU speed toggle:** `realistic` (default) or `fast` (~2s CPU picks). Affects bot think-time only — the human's clock always runs real. *(Erratum v2.13.1 — AP.3/migration 091, measured against the shipped code: this section's own new pacing rule below makes a CPU **raise** untimed by construction (it runs in the transaction that provoked it), so **after AP.3 the toggle has no raise think-time left to govern**. What it still governs is the **nomination** think-time in an auction and ARM 2.5's snake picks. The texture that is genuinely lost is a CPU raise landing inside the final anti-snipe seconds — anti-snipe is no longer demonstrated by the bots in a mock, only by a human's own late bid. That is a consequence of Chris's ruling, not a deviation from it, and it is recorded here rather than left for a reader of this bullet to discover in the code. The nomination half's own sweep latency is PROGRESS **F92**.)*

**Auction bots bid like people, and that is a pacing requirement (NEW v2.13 — Chris, 2026-08-20).** *"On other platforms the auto draft bots bid like normal people would so if a player is valued at $40, two things can happen. 1 a bot can bid $35 or $40 immediately if they want, just like a real person can. They also bid super fast in $1 increments, like real players do."*

- **Acceptance bar (Chris's numbers, from real use on Sleeper at 12 teams × 16 spots):** a **mock auction completes in under 45 minutes**, and a live auction with real managers in under 90 (§8.6.3). These are acceptance criteria for the auction, not aspirations — the mock is where a commissioner finds out whether their settings are playable, and a rehearsal nobody finishes is not a rehearsal. Measure the mock end to end (start → `in_season`) on the default league shape and record the number. *(Erratum v2.16 — **DE-TARGETED, ruled by Chris 2026-08-21:** *"My point about finishing a mock in 45 minutes was just an example not a target because previously it was 3 hours which basically makes it useless. with all the other fixes we've put in I think we're good."* **The 45-minute figure is an ILLUSTRATION of the problem AP.3 solved, not an acceptance criterion, and nothing is gated on it.** The 90-minute live-draft figure keeps the same status. What survives is the sentence's real content — **a rehearsal nobody finishes is not a rehearsal** — and the standing instruction to *measure* a mock end to end and record the number, which stays useful precisely because it is no longer a pass/fail line. PROGRESS **F85** is de-targeted with it; **F92** (the CPU nomination still riding the 5-second sweep) survives on its own merits as a pacing cost worth fixing rather than as a term in a bar.)*
- **A CPU raise is provoked by the bid it answers, never by the sweep.** The `draft-tick` sweep runs every ~5s across every live draft in the system and must stay that way (§8.2/§22.3) — but riding it made each $1 raise cost 5 seconds of wall clock, so walking a $1 opening up to a $40 clearing price took 39 raises ≈ 3m15s of nothing happening. CPU responses therefore run **in the transaction that provoked them** (a human's bid, a raise, or the nomination that opened the market), bounded and deterministic; the sweep keeps the job it is genuinely good at — **expiring clocks when nobody acts**. Do not make the global sweep faster to solve a mock-pacing problem.
- **Jump-bids within value.** A CPU raises to any amount up to `min(its computed value, its max bid)` — a seeded choice between the $1 nibble and a jump — rather than always laddering `+$1`. Both textures are required: the jump is how a real manager says *"I'll pay $40 for him"*, the nibble is how a real room grinds a price up. Bidding **above** its computed value stays impossible, and every CPU bid still passes the same max-bid/solvency validator a human's does.
- **This supersedes the shipped `+$1`-once-per-sweep behaviour** (PROGRESS D132's letter, F76's arithmetic, question Q15). The value model itself is unchanged.
- **What does NOT change:** the human's clock always runs real (this section's own rule). Nothing here shortens a bid window, and the `fast` toggle still touches bot think-time only. A mock is slow when the league's clocks are slow, and that is correct — mock fidelity means a commissioner who sets a 60-second bid clock finds out what a 60-second bid clock feels like.
- **Zero side effects:** no `league_rosters`, no `transactions`, no league status transitions, no notifications to other members. Picks exist only under the mock `draft_id`; chat scoped to the mock room. Mock parameters live in `drafts.config.mock = { human_team_id, cpu_speed, launched_by, action_id? }` — no schema change (§12.3 already has `is_mock`). *(Erratum v2.8.16 — `launched_by` added as the D103(2) authorization key: the launcher is the mock's only legal human driver (pick on the human seat, pause/resume, delete), keyed on this value and never on seat ownership; ~~every §8.7 commissioner control refuses mocks~~ **— AMENDED v2.15, see the block below: the launcher holds them. The reason this clause existed is not amended:** an unguarded reset was a live-proven league write. Deleting a mock (owner or the expiry cron) explicitly removes its `draft:<mock_id>` chat rows — the chat FK is to the league, so CASCADE cannot reach them. Completed 2026-08-04, M2 batch 7 R149: an optional `action_id` — the launch-idempotency key (one UUID per client submit); a replayed submit returns the original mock instead of a second one, launcher-scoped, absent when the caller sends none.)*
- **Lifecycle:** pause/leave anytime (auto-pauses on disconnect); resumable from the league page; abandoned mocks auto-expire after **72h** (daily cleanup cron); a finished mock keeps a **recap** (full board + your roster vs. the CPUs') until the user deletes it. Cap: **3 active mocks per user** (§22.5). *(Erratum v2.8.16 — mechanics: the auto-pause fires when the LAUNCHER's heartbeat is stale past `disconnect_grace_seconds` + one tick (D93), always before the §8.5.5 grace hold could autopick the disconnected human; resume is the launcher's action, never the tick's. "Idle" for the 72h expiry = GREATEST(the mock's `updated_at`, the launcher's last heartbeat) — either signal resets the clock; completed recaps never expire.)*
- **Load accounting:** a mock is one realtime subscriber and rides the same SKIP-LOCKED `draft-tick`; cheap, but counted in the §22 draft-concurrency budget and load tests.
- **v1 scope:** ~~league-attached only (config, roster shape, and the player pool come from the league). A standalone practice lobby with quick settings~~ — **AMENDED v2.16, and the amendment REVERSES the priority: the STANDALONE practice mock is v1, and the LEAGUE-ATTACHED one is deferred** (Chris, 2026-08-21). A mock owns its settings; the player pool is global and never came from the league. **League inheritance is deferred on sequencing, not merit** — Chris rates it highly (*"a really awesome feature that current platforms fail to do effectively"*) — and returns when the league product has demand. **Multi-human mock rooms remain v1.1**, unamended: that half is about a solo practice's authority model (§8.8's v2.15 block), not about where settings come from.

**The mock is your sandbox: the launcher holds the commissioner's tools (NEW v2.15 — Chris, 2026-08-20).** *"in a mock my assumption was that the solo person is the commish so the user running the mock should be able to make it as fast as possible IMO and pause it if they need to do something. that's kind of the point of these mocks…"* · *"these mocks just get deleted when they're done so it really doesn't matter. if anything it could be a good way for random people to know what tools a commish has."*

- **The launcher is the commissioner of their own mock.** Every §8.7 control the launcher may reach acts on the mock, keyed on `config.mock.launched_by` and on nothing else. This **amends** the v2.8.16 erratum above, which closed the whole surface. That erratum was right about *authority* — a §8.7 control on somebody else's practice is interference — and it caught a real bug; what it missed is that **in a mock the launcher and the commissioner are the same person**, so there is nobody to interfere with.
- **D103(2)'s no-other-driver doctrine is UNCHANGED and is not what this amends.** A league commissioner who did not launch the mock gets **nothing** — no bypass, no override, no visibility into the controls. Authority moves to the launcher; it is not shared with anyone.
- **§8.8's zero-side-effects rule is the GATE on every control, unweakened.** A mock still writes nothing to the real league: no `league_rosters`, no `transactions`, no league status transitions, no notifications, no writes to `leagues`/`league_members`/`teams`, and no broadcast on the `league:<id>` topic. **Which controls can satisfy that is settled by MEASUREMENT, per control, not by reading the code** — a whole-schema delta plus a whole-row composite over the real league's rows, taken around each enabled action (a row count cannot see an in-place UPDATE, and several of these controls perform one). **A control that cannot pass stays refused, and its refusal says why IT specifically is refused** — the class-wide sentence "mock drafts have no commissioner controls" retires with this entry, because it stops being true.
- **PARITY IS THE RULE, AND IT OVERRIDES THE SAFETY INSTINCT (Chris, 2026-08-20):** *"so the point is the commish will need to set the draft order no matter, and it can always change last second. so there needs to be a way for the commish to set the order and then if real leagues can a mock league should as well so everyone can simulate their actual draft position or test different ones if they have a choice. for example, in my league last place gets to choose their draft position the next year."* **A mock gets whatever a real league gets — not whatever is safe to give it.** A mock that cannot reproduce the user's actual draft position is not practice, it is a different draft; this section's own *"Same engine, literally"* promise, extended from the engine to the commissioner surface. So **a control that fails this section's isolation rule is FIXED where fixing it is possible, and refused only where it writes the real league by construction** or where its product value is genuinely served another way. **Concretely: the launcher can set their mock's draft order** (and, in an auction, its nomination order), and **can choose their starting slot when they launch it** — 1..N, default random, the other franchises shuffled around them, written to the mock's own order and requiring no real order to exist (*"there is no real draft order, most drafts will set the order right before"*). **A draft order is negotiated property** — *"two people text messaging agree to swap picks"* — so a write that silently reverses one is a serious defect, not a cosmetic one, and the isolation pin for these controls is over the **real draft's stored order**, not over a row count. *(The commissioner's two-slot **swap** — "oh you like the 7th pick i like the 3rd pick" — is a separate, real-league gap: today's editor is a move-and-shift that displaces bystanders. It is PROGRESS **F101**, is not part of this entry, and acquires **no** trade system: the agreement happens off-platform, so the commissioner simply executes and a room line records it.)*
- **Known refusals at the time of this entry (three, not one).** **`draft_reset`** writes `leagues.status` and strips the stored auto-start instant — the live-proven breach v2.8.16 was written for; whether a mock-safe variant replaces it is task MS.4's decision, and if built it must return the mock to **`live` at pick 1** (a `scheduled` mock cannot be restarted — `draft_start_internal` excludes mocks). **The autopick toggle** (§8.7's "Toggle autopick for any team") writes `league_members.is_autodraft` on the real seat by construction, and a mock never reads that flag anyway. **Seat reassignment** reaches the league's real invite/assignment surface. None of the three gets a mock-scoped shadow of a league table — §8.8's v1 scope is league-attached on purpose. **A FOURTH control failed this section's rule and is FIXED rather than refused: "Edit draft order" is MIS-TARGETED, not blocked** — its request carries no draft id, so the service resolves the league's *real* active draft and the RPC's own mock refusal is never reached. Per the parity bullet above it is corrected at the service layer so a mock room targets its own draft; refusing it was the wrong remedy and would have cost the user the ability to practise from their real slot. **The general rule this establishes: a control is proven mock-safe only when it has been driven through the surface a user actually reaches it by, with a real draft alive in the same league** — an RPC-level measurement alone cannot see a mis-target, and a clean RPC-level composite is not a clean bill of health.
- **The tools are present, not explained (RULED — Chris, 2026-08-20: "no").** No teaching label, no tutorial, no onboarding hint, no first-run tip. Discovery is a *consequence* of the tools being there, which is what his second sentence observes; it is not a feature built on top of them. The requirement is that a launcher can **find** the controls where commissioner controls live (§16.4's command bar), not that anything explains them. Control labels and refusal messages say what a control does or why it refused — that is §16.5.4's designed-copy rule and is not "explanation".
- **Governing note for the whole area (Chris, 2026-08-20):** *"a commish can pause a draft and change anything or someone can disconnect at any time. TBH in drafts that's known as a 'personal problem'."* No defensive machinery is specced for humans changing their minds or dropping connection. This does not touch the engine's correctness guards — solvency, the draft-row lock, idempotency and this section's isolation rule are the contract, not "defence".
- **Task lane:** `docs/specs/tasks-MS-mock-sandbox.md` (prefix `MS.`, eight tasks); PROGRESS **D216–D223**, **F98–F101**, **E75–E77**.

**A mock draft is for practicing, and it is a STANDALONE thing (NEW v2.16 — Chris, 2026-08-21).** *"a user may run a mock draft without even being 'in a league'. i'm not sure why you are so focused on making a mock draft connected to a specific leagues settings."* · *"what if we just start by only having mocks that run off the base scoring templates and don't inherit any of the custom league logic"* · *"we can get back to the league based mocks after we actually have demand for the league product itself. until then we can invite users to come do mocks for practice."*

- **THE PREMISE THIS AMENDS.** Since v2.3 this section has described a mock as a **rehearsal of one specific league**, and every mechanism follows honestly from it. **Practice is the purpose; a league is optional context.** The reason the old premise held is the workflow it assumed: **a friends league settles its settings by group text and enters them *"when they are all in the draft lobby together"***, so the moment someone most wants to practise is *before* any league on the platform has the settings in it. The premise assumed the league arrives first; in the real workflow it arrives last.
- **A MOCK IS STANDALONE. FULL STOP.** No league, no inherited settings, no hidden container standing in for one. **Its settings come from the six base scoring templates** *(v2.16.9, marked up by Chris 2026-08-31: **seven** once Scout Scoring ships — the launcher preselects Scout Scoring as the system default, still freely changeable; §7.3.3 / App B.5)* *(v2.16.11, marked up 2026-09-01: **eight** with the Scout pair — the launcher, unfiltered, still preselects the system default, Scout Standard; App B.5.1)* (§7.3.3 / App B — ESPN Standard & Full PPR, Sleeper Standard & Full PPR, Yahoo Standard & Half PPR, already shipped) **plus this spec's own draft defaults**, and **the launcher changes them at launch**: *"just let the user make the clocks what they want. Use the same default … Everyone is going change the clock times so the defaults are merely a suggestion."*
- **THE INHERITED THREAT MODEL DOES NOT APPLY, AND THIS IS THE ENTRY'S LOAD-BEARING RULE.** Mock Draft Mode was built inside the leagues system and inherited its whole protective apparatus — RLS keyed on league membership, this section's zero-side-effects isolation, commissioner authority, audit immutability. **Every one of those exists because a real draft has twelve humans with money at stake: an adversary, a dispute, a record that must survive. A mock has one human and eleven bots.** Chris put it plainly: *"I doubt they are doing any of this inivisible league blah blah blah security container stuff."* **Two obligations survive, and neither is about adversaries: (a) a user must not be able to break their own practice irrecoverably** — concretely, the bot seats a mock mints must not be deletable out from under a live draft, which would wedge it at that seat's turn with no in-product recovery — **and (b) one user's practice is not another's to read or drive, which is ONE OWNERSHIP PREDICATE (`config.mock.launched_by`), not a membership graph.** **A mechanism that cannot name the party it protects whom from does not belong in Mock Draft Mode.**
- **ZERO SIDE EFFECTS IS UNWEAKENED AND ITS SCOPE IS STATED EXACTLY.** For a **league-attached** mock every clause of this section's zero-side-effects rule keeps its full force. For a **standalone** mock there is no league to have an effect on, so the obligation becomes *nothing leaks into the league product* — no `leagues`, `league_members`, `league_rosters` or `transactions` row, no notification, no broadcast on any `league:<id>` topic — **and that is established by measurement (a whole-schema delta across the practice's lifecycle), never asserted.**
- **NO MOCK REFUSES TO START FOR WANT OF HUMANS.** A standalone mock mints its own CPU opponents. The D103(1) full-seat-map rule was a statement about borrowing a league's franchises and has no subject here.
- **TWO ENTRY POINTS — one ships, one is deferred.** **(1) From the FieldScout Home tab**, on the base templates — **this is v1.** **(2) From a League Home, beside the real draft-lobby button, inheriting that league's exact settings** — *"this one inheriting your league settings is actually a really awesome feature that current platforms fail to do effectively"* — **deferred, not cancelled.** **Design obligation on the shipping half: the launch path takes a settings OBJECT and does not care where it came from, so inheritance later is a new SOURCE, not a rewrite.**
- **THE MOCK DRAFT REPORT, AND WHERE IT LIVES.** *"it would also be great to have a Mock Draft Report that lives somewhere, that is basically a table of every player from the draft and their cost and what team they went to."* A completed mock keeps a **flat table** alongside its board and rosters: **one table with two column sets, never one table with blanks** — an auction report is *player · position · team · **price** · **nomination #***, a snake/linear report is *player · position · team · **round** · **pick #***, because an empty column claims a value exists and is unknown, which is false in both directions. **Mock reports live on the USER, not on the league** (*"mocks should go to something on the User's profile, not at the league level. However, real drafts should have a place at the league level"*) — a user-scoped list of every past mock, reachable from a **Mock Drafts** entry in the app's **More…** section, each row opening its report. **The league-level recap keeps real drafts and is otherwise unchanged**, and any existing mock-recap link keeps working by redirect, never by 404.
- **MOCKS ARE INDEPENDENT OF LEAGUE WORK (RULED — *"Mocks do not need to be connected to League work"*).** The practice surfaces carry **their own release gate**, never the leagues gate: **turning leagues off must not turn practice off**, and no practice work is blocked on league work. **This is a RELEASE statement, not a code-isolation one** — a mock still runs on the shared draft engine (`drafts`, `draft-tick`, the room, the same RPC family, the same suites), that sharing is correct and stays, and it is what makes the standalone mock affordable at all. **Independence is a claim about ROUTES as well as components:** a practice surface living under a league-gated route path is gated whatever its own flag says, so the practice room, its list and its report carry a route family of their own, keyed on the **mock's** id. The gate covers **UI surfaces only** — never a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC, whose authorization stays in-body and server-authoritative (§12).
- **THE CLOCKS, RULED DIRECTLY AND IDENTICAL FOR MOCKS AND REAL LEAGUES:** nomination **30s**, bid clock **20s**, and a bid landing inside the last **10s** re-floors the clock to 10s (anti-snipe). **There is no mock-specific default of any kind** — *"the default clock time shouldn't change for mocks … Use the same default."* This section's fidelity promise — *"A mock is slow when the league's clocks are slow, and that is correct"* — **stands unamended and is dormant while league inheritance is deferred**; it is exactly the rule that half returns with. *(PROGRESS **Q23** — whether a mock-specific bid clock would override a league's chosen one — **lapses**: the design changed underneath it and there is no mock-specific default to override with.)*
- **This is launch-facing.** *"until then we can invite users to come do mocks for practice."* The practice surfaces ship at the redesign's quality bar — loading, empty, error and overflow states included (§16.5) — and still carry **no explanatory copy**: labels say what a control does, an empty state says what is empty and what to do, and nothing teaches the user what a mock draft is.
- **Task lane:** `docs/specs/tasks-MP-mock-practice.md` (prefix `MP.`, eleven tasks); PROGRESS **D225–D233**, **F102–F108**, **Q24**, **E78–E80**.

### 8.9 Draft references: your league-tagged lists in the room
The Live Draft Tool surfaces the user's league-tagged lists (§7.4) so their prep is usable on the clock.

- **"My Lists" panel** in the draft room (a tab beside *My Queue*): shows every list the user attached to this league, any **league-shared** lists, and their season Big Board by default. *(Erratum v2.16.4 — **what the panel shows in a STANDALONE practice room**, which v2.16.3 deliberately left open (task MP.6c; PROGRESS D247(9)/F122): **the launcher's OWN lists, Big Board first, and nothing else** — the same ownership predicate v2.16.3 gave the verb, so the panel can never offer what the load would refuse. There are no attachments and no fellow members without a league, so **attach / detach / set-primary do not appear**, and **the best-available helper below does not render either** — a primary board is an ATTACHMENT flag (§7.4), so a room with no league has none. **The league arm is unchanged, byte for byte.**)*
- **Cheat sheet:** open any attached list (ranked, with tiers) in a side panel while drafting.
- **Overlay on the pool:** toggle a list as an overlay on Available Players — show each player's rank/tier from that list as a column, and/or **filter the pool to "only players on this list."**
- **Load into queue:** one tap to **load an attached list into the draft queue** in list order (skipping already-drafted players), or "Add remaining" to append; reorder afterward as normal. *(Erratum v2.16.3 — **this whole section scopes loadable lists by the LEAGUE TAG, and a STANDALONE practice draft has no league to tag one to** (task MP.6b; §8.8's v2.16 block). The server rule on the standalone arm is therefore **OWNERSHIP: a launcher may load a list they OWN, and nothing else** — §8.8 v2.16's *"ONE OWNERSHIP PREDICATE, not a membership graph"* applied to the second table the verb touches. It is deliberately **narrower** than the league arm rather than wider: a **public** list another user owns is readable and is still refused, because admitting it would be a rule no ruling asked for, and widening later is additive. **Nothing about the league arm changes** — attached or league-shared, exactly as printed above, byte for byte. **What this erratum does NOT settle is which lists the standalone room OFFERS** (the panel above is a league surface); that is the room's question and is carried as PROGRESS **F122**.)*
- **Best available from my board:** an optional helper highlights the highest-ranked still-available player from the user's primary board.
- **Autopick tie-in:** if the user set a primary draft board (§7.4), autopick uses it before the generic Big Board (§8.4).
- **Live:** as players come off the board, overlays, queue, and "best available" update via the same `draft:<id>` Realtime channel.
- Mobile: the panel is a bottom sheet.

## 9. Real-time Architecture

Builds on the existing Supabase Realtime usage (`docs/02-TECHNICAL-ARCHITECTURE.md` §Real-time Features) — **revised in v2.0 for scale.** The v1.x design subscribed clients to **Postgres Changes** on league tables. Supabase's own guidance now warns against this at scale: Postgres Changes authorizes **every change against every subscriber** (1 write × 100 subscribers = 100 RLS checks) and processes all changes on a **single thread** to preserve ordering — throughput scales with subscriber count, not write rate, and larger compute doesn't help. With hundreds of leagues live on a Sunday, that design falls over. Supabase's recommended replacement is **Broadcast from Database**: DB triggers call `realtime.broadcast_changes()` to publish to **private, authorized Broadcast channels**, which scales to tens of thousands of concurrent subscribers.

**v2.0 transport rule: clients never use Postgres Changes. All authoritative table events reach clients via Broadcast-from-Database triggers on private channels.**

| Mechanism | Used for | Authoritative? |
|---|---|---|
| **Broadcast from Database** (triggers → `realtime.broadcast_changes()`) | The truth: `drafts` state, `draft_picks`, `draft_bids`, `matchups` score updates, `team_week_results`, `transactions`, `league_rosters`, `commissioner_actions`, `league_chat`, `lineup_swaps` | **Yes** — but clients treat each event as a *cache-invalidation + payload hint*; on any doubt they refetch via REST |
| **Presence** | Who is in the draft room / online; per-team connection status | No (ephemeral) |
| **Client Broadcast** | Ephemeral UX only: "typing…", bid-button pulses, optimistic intent echoes | No (ephemeral) |

### 9.1 Channels (all private; Realtime Authorization via RLS on `realtime.messages`)
- `draft:<draft_id>` — draft state, picks, bids, draft chat, Presence, a 15s server heartbeat for clock-drift correction. Fan-out ≤ 20 clients + spectators.
- `league:<league_id>` — matchup/score updates, transactions, roster changes, commissioner actions, league chat, swap events. Powers the activity feed, live standings, and the audit log.
- **No client subscribes to `player_stats` or `nfl_games`.** Raw stat deltas are ingested server-side and fanned out per league by the `score-league-week` worker (§22.2), which writes `matchups` / `team_week_results` and lets the table triggers broadcast one compact `scores_updated` event per league per batch. Per-player box-score lines are fetched via REST when a matchup view is open, re-fetched on `scores_updated`.

### 9.2 Trigger + authorization pattern
```sql
-- One trigger per authoritative table, e.g.:
CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION broadcast_draft_changes() RETURNS trigger
LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = '' AS $$
BEGIN
  PERFORM realtime.broadcast_changes(
    'draft:' || COALESCE(NEW.draft_id, OLD.draft_id)::text,  -- topic
    TG_OP, TG_OP, TG_TABLE_NAME, TG_TABLE_SCHEMA, NEW, OLD);
  RETURN NULL;
END $$;
CREATE TRIGGER tr_broadcast_draft_picks
  AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE ON draft_picks
  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION broadcast_draft_changes();

-- Authorization: members may read their league/draft topics.
CREATE POLICY "members read league topics" ON realtime.messages
  FOR SELECT TO authenticated USING (
    (SELECT realtime.topic()) LIKE 'league:%'
    AND is_league_member(split_part((SELECT realtime.topic()), ':', 2)::uuid)
  );
-- (equivalent policy for 'draft:%' topics via drafts.league_id lookup)
```
- Column-select payloads: broadcast only the columns clients render (never blind-bid amounts, never private queue rows). Sensitive tables (`waiver_claims`, `draft_queues`) are **never broadcast** — owners poll/refetch their own rows.
- `realtime.send()` is used for synthetic events with no backing row (e.g., `scores_updated { week, updated_team_ids }`).

### 9.3 Client rules (unchanged doctrine, stronger wording)
- On join/reconnect: **fetch authoritative state via REST first**, render, *then* subscribe. Never depend on missed broadcasts. Each broadcast carries a monotonic `state_version` (from `drafts.updated_at`/sequence); a gap ⇒ refetch.
- Countdowns render from `current_deadline` (server timestamp) + a clock offset measured at subscribe (and corrected by the 15s heartbeat). Clients never own the clock.
- All writes go through Route Handlers/RPCs. The only direct client INSERT is `league_chat` (RLS-scoped, rate-limited).
- Subscription budget: **one socket per client, ≤ 3 channels** (current league, current draft, notifications). Unsubscribe on route change — connection leaks are the #1 cause of Realtime quota suspensions.
- **One live room per browser, per draft (v2.12 — the two-tabs guard; RULED by Chris 2026-08-17: *"The most recent one takes over and the others disconnect."*).** Desktop entry opens the room in a new browser tab (§16.1), so a user can trivially end up with two tabs of the same draft: two sockets, two `draft:<id>` subscriptions, two presence heartbeats under one presence key, and two `draft_touch` liveness loops — a per-user doubling of the fan-out this section's budget is written to bound, and a presence list that shows one manager twice. **The newest tab of a given draft holds the connection; older tabs release it** — they unsubscribe, stop the heartbeat, and render a plain "This draft is open in another tab" state with a **Use this tab instead** button that takes the connection back. Coordination is client-local (a `BroadcastChannel` keyed by draft id, `localStorage` as the fallback) and deliberately **per browser profile, not per user**: a manager legitimately watching on a laptop and a phone is two clients and is left alone. The guard is a **courtesy over a correctness boundary, never a substitute for one** — the server stays authoritative, so a stale tab that acts is refused by the same validators as anyone else (§8.1).

### 9.4 Capacity notes (see §22 for full model)
Supabase enforces per-plan quotas on concurrent connections, messages/sec, channel joins/sec, and channels per connection; overages bill per 1k peak connections and per 1M messages. The §22 capacity model shows the target load (hundreds of concurrent leagues) fits comfortably **only** with the Broadcast-from-DB design and per-league score batching; Postgres Changes would not. Escape hatches if growth exceeds managed quotas: Supabase Team plan custom limits → self-hosted Realtime (open source) → dedicated realtime provider for the socket layer only.
