As marked up the rules object carries **43 coefficients** (14 offense/turnover/return + 5 kicking + 7 D/ST events + 8 ESPN PA tiers + 9 YA tiers; `return_yards` excluded until F71). *(The draft counted 32 on its single-family pick; Chris's 2026-08-31 rulings add `fg_missed`, replace the 7-key shared PA family with the 8-key ESPN family, and add the 9-key YA table — recounted from the table above, not assumed.)*

**The roster half — a SEPARATE surface, deliberately.** Starts **1 QB / 2 RB / 3 WR / 1 TE / 1 FLEX (W/R/T) / 1 K / 1 D/ST** (10 starters), **bench 6**, **1 IR spot** *(type unrestricted, OUT/IR designations — **APPROVED as drafted (Chris, 2026-08-31)**: he said "1 IR spot"; the type and designations inherit the shipped default)*, Hot Swap off. These are `leagues.roster_settings` defaults (§7.3.2's preset table, amended in this version), **not scoring rules: the §7.3.3.1 format-2 envelope has no roster fields and must not grow any** (SE.4's envelope-shape sweep already refuses unknown members — 23 envelope shapes measured). Measured delta from today's shipped default: **exactly one number, WR 2 → 3** (`DEFAULT_ROSTER_SETTINGS` at `src/lib/leagues/settings/league-settings.ts:164`, migration 040's column DEFAULT, and §7.3.2's table already match every other value). It lands as its own task (SC.2) at §7.3.2's landing sites — never inside the scoring document.

**Why it's better — user-facing copy — APPROVED as drafted (Chris, 2026-08-31)** *(the #228 markup fold's blanket approval covered this copy — D278(8)(c), "the user-facing copy" — but the fold flipped every B.5 mark EXCEPT this heading's DRAFT-COPY mark; the leftover mark is corrected in passing, v2.16.11 — measured: the DRAFT-COPY grep → 1 before the fix, this heading at spec:2386)* (surfaces on the Scout picker card, §16.2):

> **Scout Scoring** is one clean rule set instead of a pile of inherited quirks. Six points for every touchdown, whoever scores it. Three points for every field goal, wherever it's kicked from. Yardage at flat, memorable rates. No point just for catching the ball.
> - **Every TD is 6.** A passing touchdown is worth exactly what a rushing or receiving one is — six points on the real scoreboard. The 4-point passing TD is a legacy discount.
> - **Every FG is 3.** Distance is mostly the coach's call and where the drive stalled — distance luck isn't kicker skill.
> - **No PPR.** A catch is how production starts, not production itself. Volume without yards or scores shouldn't outscore production.
> - **Balanced yardage.** 10 rushing or receiving yards to the point, 20 passing yards — passing yardage comes roughly twice as easily, so a yard is paid what it's worth.

*(Return yards deliberately absent from the copy until F71 lands — the copy never promises a gated stat.)*

Ledger row **F186**. #### B.5.1 The Scout pair — Scout Standard + Scout PPR (NEW v2.16.11 — marked up by Chris 2026-09-01)

**Status: MARKED UP by Chris 2026-09-01 — all three marks resolved; the #228-style gate has passed (PR #239 is the approval anchor; the coordinator merges it).** Task **SC.4** (ledger **F203**) is takeable once #239 merges. The resolutions, each keeping the draft's basis as the trail (the #228 fold convention): **(1) the rename RULED** — "Scout Scoring" → "Scout Standard"; **(2) the opening side RULED** — his words verbatim, *"Scout Standard should be the default selected template."*, answered at the SELECTION level, with the one-step derivation recorded at §7.3.3's system-default bullet (Scout Standard preselected ⇒ the style step opens No PPR ⇒ an explicit PPR flip preselects Scout PPR per the family law); **(3) both copy marks APPROVED as drafted** — the card one-liner and the copy below ship as written.

**Chris, 2026-08-31/09-01, verbatim (LAW for this draft):**
> "actually can we have Scout PPR and Scout Standard, so effectively two templates? for PPR our default is .2 pts per reception. does that change anything order wise?"

**What the pair is.** **Scout Standard** is the shipped 43-value body above — migration 106's seeded row, `receptions: 0`, byte-for-byte what SC.1 built and Chris marked up. **Scout PPR** is that same body with **exactly one value changed: `receptions: 0.2`** — the same 43 keys, one value apart. Same split D/ST tables, same flat FGs, same 6-point TDs, same 0.05/0.1 yardage rates, decimal on; 0.2 is a 2dp value, so the §7.3.3.1 guardrail-5 rule holds untouched. Every basis and assumption in the table above carries over unchanged — the pair introduces **no new value question** beyond the reception coefficient Chris just ruled. Coefficient census across the shipped templates: 261 → **304** (+43 — the census counts keys, not non-zeros).

**The name — RULED (Chris, 2026-09-01): "Scout Scoring" → "Scout Standard".** The shipped row is named **"Scout Scoring"**, and that name is the **natural key**: SC.3's resolver resolves the system default by it (`DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME`, `scoring-template-picker-ops.ts`), and SC.1's seed and golden pins carry it (measured: `grep -rc "Scout Scoring" src/ supabase/ --include='*.ts' --include='*.tsx' --include='*.sql'` → **23 files / 82 occurrences**). The draft takes the incumbent convention every other card already follows — ESPN Standard / ESPN Full PPR, Sleeper Standard / Sleeper Full PPR — and renames the no-PPR row **"Scout Standard"** so the pair reads as a pair. What the rename touches — enumerated for the markup, now SC.4's buildable rename leg: **(1)** the seeded row's `name` — an UPDATE by natural key in SC.4's **NEW migration** (**never an edit to 106**; the SE.4-chain 0-line-diff pins hold for 106 exactly as for 058, and the `(name) WHERE is_template` partial UNIQUE makes the rename atomic and collision-checked); **(2)** the resolver constant `DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME`; **(3)** the `TEMPLATE_DISPLAY_ORDER` + `TEMPLATE_ONE_LINERS` keys; **(4)** pgTAP 054's literals (`grep -c 'Scout Scoring' supabase/tests/054_scout_scoring_template.sql` → **17** — amended in place, dated); **(5)** pgTAP 053 §B2's string literal (the `Scout Scoring=[…]` segment — its name-sort position also shifts, see the order answer below); **(6)** the parity suite's name key (`template-parity.test.ts`'s `PINNED_TOTALS` row + docblock). **The alternative he did not take — keep "Scout Scoring" as the no-PPR row's name** (zero rename churn; kept as the trail): its cost is an **asymmetric pair** — "Scout Scoring" / "Scout PPR" reads as family-name-plus-variant rather than the Standard/PPR convention every other card follows, and the no-PPR half stops saying out loud that it IS the no-PPR half.

**Preselection law for the pair.** The **system default stays Scout Standard** — no-PPR is the FieldScout identity of the original 2026-08-26 ruling ("No PPR" is its first sentence). Under the wizard's reception-style filter, **each family preselects its own Scout**: the No-PPR view preselects Scout Standard, the PPR view preselects Scout PPR; the mock launcher (unfiltered) keeps preselecting the system default. Still a **preselection, never a silent write** — every payload carries an explicit template id exactly as SC.3 built it (D170 untouched; the settings mount still never defaults over a stored reference; a missing row still degrades loudly, naming whichever half is absent). The consequences live in §7.3.3's amended system-default bullet: **D282(3) dissolves** (no view hides a default), **the SC.3 review's swap-hazard finding retires** (the reset-refill lands on the active family's own Scout — the intended default, never a surprise substitution), and the opening side is **RULED (Chris, 2026-09-01)** — §7.3.3's system-default bullet carries his verbatim sentence and the one-step derivation. The **"FieldScout's default" marker** follows the same law: unfiltered mounts wear it on Scout Standard alone (the system default); a style-filtered view moves it to the family's own Scout — a badge on a filtered-out card marks nothing (SC.4's one-pass UI call, drafted here so it isn't improvised at build time).

**"Does that change anything order wise?" — answered on the record.** **Nothing structural reorders.** The "exactly seven" census pins were **built to move** — this is the third template addition through the same mechanism (058's six → SC.1's seventh → this pair's eighth; D278(4) measured it, D279 built it, PROGRESS D284(4) re-measures it today; the mechanism is now routine) — and everything derived moves by construction (the acceptance floor, the picker's skeleton/empty-state counts since F193's discharge, 053 §B3's byte-exact regeneration). What DOES reorder is presentation: the §7.3.3 table — which IS the picker order and the `SCORING_TEMPLATES` order — goes **Scout Standard, Scout PPR, then the six incumbents unchanged**, so each style-filtered view leads with its own Scout and the unfiltered launcher leads with the pair. One mechanical note: pgTAP 053 §B2's name-sorted literal places `Scout PPR` between `ESPN Standard` and `Scout Standard`. The one residual ordering choice — which side the style step OPENS on — was his, and is **RULED (2026-09-01)**: No PPR, derived from his default-selected-template sentence (the system-default bullet).

**Scout PPR — "why it's better" copy — APPROVED as drafted (Chris, 2026-09-01)** (surfaces on the Scout PPR picker card, §16.2):

> **Scout PPR** is the same clean rule set with one addition: **0.2 points per reception**. The catch earns something — it's how production starts — but a fifth of a point can't outscore the yards and touchdowns it's supposed to lead to. Full PPR pays a full point for volume alone and lets catch counts inflate scores; 0.2 rewards the catch without inflating it.

*(The card one-liner in §7.3.3's table row is APPROVED under the same 2026-09-01 markup. Return yards stay absent from this copy too — the copy never promises a gated stat.)*

**One measured consequence outside the picker, stated so SC.4 pins it deliberately rather than tripping on it:** the auction room's projections-column classifier (`scoringFamilyFromRules`, `auction-player-table-ops.ts` — midpoint cuts `HALF_PPR_FLOOR 0.25` / `PPR_FLOOR 0.75`, both boundaries pinned) maps **0.2 → `standard`**: a Scout PPR league's Proj / Pts-wk / $-per-point columns read `projected_pts_standard`, the **nearest** of the three synced projection families — the shipped midpoint rule's designed output (0.2 sits closer to 0 than to 0.5), not a defect. The docblock's "three shipped shapes are 0 / 0.5 / 1" claim and its census test move with it — Scout PPR is the first shipped template whose `receptions` value is not one of the three synced families, so the census gains the 0.2 cell as a deliberate pin.

**`return_yards` rides the pair.** Both rows stay gated identically: F71's future seed-update step adds `return_yards: 0.05` to **each** Scout row once the key exists (F71 annotated in the same PR as this entry). Nothing in v2.16.11 un-gates the key, the catalog flag, or the editor field.

**Build hook — the markup is FOLDED (2026-09-01); SC.4 becomes takeable the moment PR #239 merges:** working id **SC.4** (ledger **F203**): a **NEW migration** — next free number measured at task time per D161/D166 (measured after #237's mid-draft merge: `ls supabase/migrations/ | tail -1` → `107_scout_default_roster.sql` ⇒ expect **108**; **added to `supabase/HELD-FROM-PRODUCTION.txt` in the same PR** or the drift check reds, by design) — seeding Scout PPR on 058/106's natural-key pattern (**never an edit to 058 or 106**) plus the rename UPDATE (RULED 2026-09-01); the `templates.ts` eighth entry (authored as a one-key spread of Scout Standard's body so "one value apart" is structural, then golden-pinned as a full literal anyway); its own golden-pin pgTAP file (test head `055` ⇒ expect **056**); **every census pin in PROGRESS D284(4)** — the measured list, no re-hunt — including the 5 new hand-computed parity pins (35 → 40; receptions 0.2 moves ONLY the reception-delivered lines, rb +0.60 and wr_te +1.60; the QB fixture catches nothing so its pin is IDENTICAL, and K/D-ST are byte-identical to Scout Standard — cross-check the invariant, don't recompute blind), the 261 → 304 coefficient census, the 670 → 672 corpus, the 7 → 8 picker, pgTAP 012/051/053's cells, and the **per-family reshape of SC.3's preselection surfaces** (the resolver's natural-key contract changes shape — single name ⇒ the family map); the SC.3 review's one-clause comment-fix nit rides along (its briefed R-number was taken mid-task — PROGRESS D284(3) carries the finding and the measured candidate site).

## Appendix C — Claude Code Build Prompts

Copy-paste prompts in the style of `docs/05-CLAUDE-CODE-PROMPTS.md`. **Hand Claude Code one task at a time**, in order. Each assumes the repo is open and the prior task is committed.

> **v2.0 note:** these prompts predate v2.0. The delivery plan (`delivery-plan-redraft-leagues.md` §3) extends this task list with the v2.0 work (L.A0 time/stats abstractions, L.D0 schedule engine, L.D4 scoring fan-out, L.D5 locks/pool, L.E2 corrections) and adds the per-task Definition-of-Done checklists every prompt must satisfy. Use the delivery plan's sequence; use these prompts as the style reference.

### Task L.A1 — League foundation: schema + RLS
```
Build the league foundation for FieldScout. Read CLAUDE.md, docs/03-DATA-MODEL.md, and docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§12) before starting. This supersedes the thin leagues schema in spec-leagues-live-mode.md.

1. Migration supabase/migrations/0XX_leagues_foundation.sql:
   - RLS helper functions is_league_member(uuid) and is_league_commish(uuid) (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE) — §12.0
   - ALTER TABLE leagues to add: status, format, team_count CHECK IN (8,10,12,14,16)  -- v1, regular_season_weeks, playoff_teams, playoff_start_week, waiver_type, faab_budget, trade_review, trade_deadline_week, lineup_lock, settings JSONB, deleted_at — §12.1
   - Replace the old "viewable by members" policy with one using is_league_member()
   - CREATE TABLE league_members (role, team_id, is_placeholder, is_autodraft, faab_balance) + RLS + indexes — §12.2
2. Zod schemas in src/types/league.ts for all settings (§7.3) with the documented defaults and ranges; a validateLeagueSettings() util enforcing the cross-field rules in §7.3 "Validation rules".
3. API route handlers (Route Handlers, kebab-case): POST/GET /api/leagues, GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/leagues/[id], POST /api/leagues/[id]/invite, POST /api/leagues/join, member management under /api/leagues/[id]/members — §15.1. League creation and joining are free (v2.8) — no is_pro gate on any of these routes.
4. React Query hooks: src/hooks/use-leagues.ts, use-league.ts, use-league-members.ts.
5. Regenerate types: npx supabase gen types typescript.
Enforce: creator becomes commissioner + gets a team; joining is free; team_count ∈ {8,10,12,14,16}; structural settings editable only in setup/scheduled (else require a commissioner override flag).
```

### Task L.A2 — League create wizard + settings UI
```
Build the league creation wizard and settings UI. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§7, §16) and docs/06-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md.

1. src/app/(app)/leagues/page.tsx (My Leagues + Create), /leagues/new/page.tsx (wizard), /leagues/[id]/settings/page.tsx.
2. Components: league-create-wizard.tsx (steps: format → roster slots → scoring → waivers/trades → draft → invite), roster-slot-builder.tsx (per-position starter counts, "Add Custom Flex" via an eligible-position multi-select, bench count, and IR spots each configured Restricted/Unrestricted with eligible designations + min weeks, live roster_size + validation), scoring-template-picker.tsx (6 parity templates per Appendix B/v2.7; compare view; no per-category editing in v1), settings-panel.tsx, invite-panel.tsx (code/link + username/email + seat list, placeholder seats, roles).
3. Use the Zod schemas + defaults from Task L.A1. Mobile-first, dark, shadcn/ui. All ranges/defaults per §7.3.
Acceptance: create an 8/10/12/14/16-team league with valid defaults in <2 min, invite & seat managers, reach status 'scheduled'.
```

### Task L.B1 — Snake draft: engine, RPCs, timer worker
```
Build the server-authoritative snake draft engine. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§8, §9, §12.3–12.6, §14) before starting. The engine MUST be server-authoritative — clients never decide turns, time, or legality.

1. Migration: drafts, draft_picks (with UNIQUE partial index uniq_draft_player_live), draft_queues + RLS (§12.3–12.6). Store league_id on child rows for simple RLS.
2. Postgres RPCs (SECURITY DEFINER), each: SELECT drafts FOR UPDATE → validate → write → advance → set current_deadline:
   - draft_create(league_id), draft_start(draft_id), draft_make_pick(draft_id, action_id, team_id, player_id), draft_set_queue(...), draft_toggle_autodraft(...)
   - Commissioner: draft_pause, draft_resume, draft_undo(draft_id, to_pick_number), draft_force_pick(...), draft_reassign_pick(...), draft_move_player(...), draft_reset(...)
   - Autopick helper: best available from queue → user's season Big Board → players.adp, filtered to fill an open roster need.
   - Idempotency via action_id; exclusivity via the unique index; friendly error on race.
3. Edge Function supabase/functions/draft-tick + pg_cron (every ~5s while a draft is 'live'): enforce current_deadline → autopick; no-op when none live. Self-schedule after each action for tight timing.
4. Route Handlers under /api/leagues/[id]/draft/* (§15.2) calling the RPCs. Commissioner routes also insert commissioner_actions (added in Task L.E1; stub the call until then).
5. Add drafts/draft_picks to supabase_realtime publication.
Acceptance: a full snake draft runs to completion across multiple browser tabs; timeouts autopick even with all tabs closed; no duplicate players under concurrent picks.
```

### Task L.B2 — Draft room UI (snake)
```
Build the live draft room UI. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§8.5, §9, §16.2–16.3) and docs/06-DESIGN-SYSTEM.md.

1. src/app/(app)/leagues/[id]/draft/page.tsx + components/draft/*: draft-room.tsx (subscribes to draft:<id>; on mount/reconnect FETCH state via REST then subscribe), draft-board-grid.tsx, pick-clock.tsx (countdown from server current_deadline + measured offset; paused state), available-players.tsx (search/filter, Big Board/ADP/tier overlays), my-queue.tsx (@dnd-kit drag), my-roster-tracker.tsx, draft-chat.tsx, presence-bar.tsx (Supabase Presence), mock-draft-launcher.tsx + a persistent MOCK banner state in draft-room.tsx (§8.8: seat picker, CPU speed toggle, recap view; CPU opponents reuse the autopick/bot logic).
2. Use Zustand for ephemeral UI (selected player, panel state, local clock); React Query + Realtime for authoritative data. Optimistic INTENT only; authoritative RESULT from broadcast. Friendly "player just went off the board" on race.
3. Touch-friendly; WCAG AA (live-region announcements for picks/clock).
Acceptance: draft night feels instant; reconnect restores full state in <2s.
```

### Task L.B3 — Commissioner draft controls
```
Add the in-draft commissioner panel. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§8.7, §10.4).

components/draft/commish-draft-panel.tsx wired to the commissioner RPCs from Task L.B1: pause/resume, edit pick clock, undo last, undo-to-pick (cascade, with a confirm showing what will be undone), reassign/edit a pick, DRAG a drafted player from one team to another, pick-for-manager, toggle autopick for any team, reverse a won bid (auction—stub until Task L.C1), reassign a draft seat, reset draft (hard confirm). Distinct accent; every action posts a non-disable-able system message to draft chat. Co-commissioners have identical access. (Audit insert lands in Task L.E1.)
Acceptance: commissioner can fix any mid-draft problem live; the room sees each action happen.
```

### Task L.B4 — Attach lists to leagues + draft-room access
```
Tie ranking lists to leagues and surface them in the draft room. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§7.4, §8.9, §12.15) before starting.

1. Migration: league_lists (UNIQUE(league_id,list_id,owner_id); partial unique index = one primary board per member per league) + RLS (own or league-shared readable; owner manages). Add league_lists to the supabase_realtime publication. Add the optional lists SELECT policy so a shared *private* list is readable by league members.
2. API + hooks: POST/GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/leagues/[id]/lists; POST /api/leagues/[id]/draft/queue/from-list/[listId]; src/hooks/use-league-lists.ts.
3. UI — attach: components/leagues/attach-list-modal.tsx (attach a list, set primary board, toggle share); entry points from list detail/card/Big Board and the league page; smart "Attach to {league}" suggestion on scoring/format match; an attach step in the create-league wizard.
4. UI — draft room: components/draft/my-lists-panel.tsx (tab beside My Queue) — attached + league-shared + season Big Board; open as cheat sheet; toggle overlay on available-players (rank/tier column + "only players on my list" filter); one-tap "Load into queue" / "Add remaining"; "best available from my primary board" highlight.
5. Autopick: update strategy to queue → primary league-tagged board → season Big Board → ADP (§8.4).
Acceptance: attach a list, set it primary, load it into the queue, overlay it on the pool, and confirm autopick uses the primary board; a shared list shows to all members; overlays/queue update live as players are drafted (E17).
```

### Task L.C1 — Auction draft
```
Add the auction draft mode. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§8.6, §12.5).

1. Migration: draft_bids (store league_id) + RLS + index.
2. RPCs: draft_nominate(draft_id, action_id, team_id, player_id, opening_bid), draft_place_bid(draft_id, action_id, team_id, amount) with budget + max-bid validation (`remaining − (open_slots−1) × reserve`, reserve per §8.6.1/v2.13) and anti-snipe (reset bid clock to auction_anti_snipe_seconds); extend draft-tick to close bids / advance nomination on timeout. Commissioner: reverse a won bid, edit a team's remaining budget (audited).
3. UI: components/draft/auction-block.tsx (current nomination, bid input, per-team budgets + max-bid, nomination & bid clocks, anti-snipe indicator).
Acceptance: full auction with no overspend; every team completes a legal roster; anti-snipe works; commissioner can reverse a won bid.
```

### Task L.D1 — In-season core: rosters, lineups, scoring, standings
```
Build in-season play. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§11, §12.7–12.8, §12.13, §14) and reuse Live Mode patterns (spec-leagues-live-mode.md); scoring uses the §7.3.3 generic calculator in src/lib/leagues/scoring/ (built in M1, L.A1.8) against the league scoring snapshot — never the legacy src/lib/scoring/default.ts (research surfaces only; erratum v2.7.1).

1. Migration: league_rosters (UNIQUE(league_id,player_id) for exclusivity; ir_placed_week, ir_lock_until_week), matchups, lineup_swaps (§12.16); ALTER team_lineups (slot_map, locked_at, edited_by_commish). Populate league_rosters from draft_picks on draft completion (status → in_season). Enforce IR slot rules (§7.3.2): placement requires an eligible designation; Restricted IR blocks removal until ir_lock_until_week (commissioner override allowed).
2. Schedule generation: round-robin across regular_season_weeks ~~honoring divisions~~ *(v2.16.12: divisions cut from v1 — Q30)* → matchups rows, mapped onto the NFL calendar per §11.7 Mid-season entry (Q29/Q31). Tiebreaker-ordered standings util (§7.3.7).
3. Lineup editor (slot-based, lock-aware per lineup_lock), lineup-lock cron, matchup live scoring (Live Mode patterns), finalize-matchups cron (respect overrides; no double-credit on stat corrections), standings + playoff bracket (byes/reseed). Swap spot auto-sub: assign one same-position bench player, arm at lock (first Sunday / Thursday if a Thursday player is involved), and a process-swaps worker that fires pre-game and in-game swaps (§7.3.2/§12.16/§14).
4. Routes/hooks per §15.3. Add tables to realtime publication.
Acceptance: simulate a week end-to-end (set → lock → live → finalize → standings); playoffs generate.
```

### Task L.D2 — Waivers/FAAB, free agency
```
Build waivers, FAAB, and free agency. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§13.1–13.2, §12.10).

1. Migration: waiver_claims (blind; own/commish RLS). FAAB balance on league_members.
2. RPC roster_add_drop (exclusivity, legality, caps, lock). Edge Function process-waivers (per-league cadence): resolve FAAB (high bid; tiebreaker; cascading per-team groups) or priority modes atomically → transactions + league_rosters; decrement faab_balance.
3. UI: free-agents-table.tsx (+ FAAB bid modal), pending claims view.
Acceptance: blind bids hidden pre-process; ties resolve deterministically; balances update; exclusivity holds.
```

### Task L.D3 — Trades
```
Build the trade system. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§13.3, §12.9, §12.11).

1. Migration: trades, trade_items, transactions (if not already). 
2. RPCs: trade_propose, trade_respond (accept/reject/cancel/vote), trade_execute (atomic roster swap, exclusivity, FAAB transfer if allowed) honoring trade_review (none/commissioner/league_vote) + trade_review_period_hours; trade-review-expiry cron; trade_deadline_week enforcement.
3. UI: trade-builder.tsx (two-sided, legality preview), trade center page, vote UI.
Acceptance: a trade completes under each review mode; deadline enforced.
```

### Task L.E1 — Commissioner Console + Audit Log (the differentiator)
```
Build the commissioner override system and the immutable, member-visible audit log. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§10, §12.12) carefully — this is the product's key differentiator.

1. Migration: commissioner_actions (reason CHECK length>0; SELECT for all members; INSERT only by commissioners; NO update/delete policies = immutable). Add matchups.override_action_id and transactions.related_action_id FKs.
2. A logCommissionerAction() helper that every override RPC calls IN THE SAME TRANSACTION as the state change, so no state change can occur without a log entry. Refactor the commissioner RPCs from L.B3/L.C1/L.D* to call it.
3. Override RPCs + routes (§15.4), each requiring a reason and writing before/after: commish_edit_score, commish_set_result, commish_move_player, commish_force_add_drop, commish_edit_lineup (any week), commish_force_or_reverse_trade, commish_edit_faab, commish_edit_record, commish_edit_schedule, commish_change_setting (optional rescore), reassign team, promote/demote.
4. UI: commish-console.tsx (tabbed hub §10.1), commish-action-modal.tsx (reason-required + before/after confirm wrapper used everywhere), illegal-lineup-flow.tsx (§10.2: flag → remedy → apply+log+announce), audit-log.tsx (filterable, human-readable, diff viewer, "✸ commissioner" badges; auto system chat posts that cannot be disabled).
5. Wire the "✸ adjusted by commissioner" badge onto any overridden matchup/score/roster, linking to the log entry.
Acceptance (all must pass): every override writes exactly one immutable log row readable by all members; changing a head-to-head result via the illegal-lineup flow works and is visible with reason + diff; attempting to UPDATE/DELETE a log row is denied for everyone; a state change can never be written without its log entry.
```

### Task L.F1 — Polish & hardening
```
Polish the leagues feature. Read docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md (§18 Phase F, §19).

Draft-order reveal animation, notifications wiring (league_invite, trade_proposal, commissioner action, waiver result), History Mode tab, optional hash-chain tamper-evidence on commissioner_actions (prev_hash/row_hash), auto-sub inactives (opt-in), public league SEO page. Then: Playwright E2E for full snake draft, full auction, disconnect/reconnect, commissioner overrides, a scored week, waiver run, and a trade; a 14-team concurrency load test of the draft room; run the design:accessibility-review and a security-review of the RLS/RPC surface. Fix what they find.
```

`result` stays `'tie'` (E38's letter holds — only the advancement reads the seed). **(B)** *"in a 2 week playoff round the scores are added together and the winner is the team with the most points over both weeks"* — the round's verdict is the SUM, each week's row keeps its own W/L/T; an equal sum falls to (A) (the recorded reading, pinned so it reds if ruled otherwise). **(C)** NO bracket for a `total_points` league — the points race IS the playoff; `playoff_teams > 0` + `total_points` is REFUSED at settings time (the two RPCs, the validator, the panel — no table CHECK: a stored pair reads as a points race, the engine reads the mode); the per-week mode is the recorded return path (F255). **(D)** with `playoff_teams = 0` (and every `total_points` league) the champion is standings RANK 1 THROUGH THE FULL CHAIN at the instant the last regular-season week goes `final`, when the league flips `in_season → complete`; `leagues.champion_team_id` written then. **(E)** *"i would build the playoff bracket immediately and then rebuild it after the final stat correction is made"* → corrected: *"no, the bracket should be built at Midnight PST when players go on waivers. which should be the same for all other weeks, no?"* / *"it should technically be Tuesday morning not Monday night."* / *"for central and eastern timezones it's already hours into Tuesday"* — the bracket is (re)built at the week's ROLLOVER, `nfl_weeks.last_game_ends_at` (the Q34(B) instant — the EVENT, not a clock; NOT Wednesday's `starts_at` ("when `playoff_start_week` arrives" struck). Plus *"the way most platforms work is they have a tab called 'playoffs' with a bracket all season that shows what the bracket would be if the playoffs started today"* — the bracket is a SEASON-LONG PROJECTED VIEW; the engine exposes it as a read all season and the same code materialises the real one at the last regular-season week's rollover. - **v2.16.20 (2026-09-05):** **PATCH — Q34(A) fold-in, an ERRATUM (the Q10/Q30/Q32-application precedent: RULED by Chris 2026-09-05 in-session via the orchestrator and applied on the L.D1.5b PR — PROGRESS §3 Q34(A) verbatim, D311, F230 discharged): `lineup_lock = first_game_of_week` is RETIRED; `per_player_kickoff` becomes the only lineup lock.** *"A player's lineup slot locks at his own kickoff — a whole-week freeze triggered by the league's first game is not a real fantasy convention and no platform implements it."* Chris, scoped to this question: *"players lock by their own kickoff not by anything else. there is no league or team wide locking."* Provenance, verified by a seven-reader investigation with adversarial fact-checks (2026-09-05): the identifier entered in the spec's creation commit `a7ff30a` (2026-07-02) and was untouched for 62 days until migration 112 built it; no changelog entry, decision row or ruling discusses it across 82 changelog entries; its sole provenance is the competitor-parity cell at App A.1, and §2.2's research summary endorses only "individual-game lineup lock by default"; every platform checked (ESPN, Yahoo, Sleeper, NFL, CBS, MFL, Fleaflicker) locks per player at his own kickoff and none at the week's first game. Sites: §7.3.6's row (single value; the rule stated); §11.2's lock bullet (the per-player sentences are the whole law; the whole-week sentence struck) and its IR bullet (R736's current-week rule RESTATED without the mode as its reason — it judges against the player's own current-week kickoff); §13.1's annotation (the pending-mode sentence replaced; the "only under `first_game_of_week` does the slot stay empty" sentence struck — an emptied slot re-seats only with an unstarted player); App A.1's parity cell struck with the reason. - **v2.16.19 (2026-09-03):** **PATCH — §13.1's `player_game_lock = off` residual now states its MEASURED consequence (PR #254 re-review, R759; no behaviour change).** The v2.16.18 text said a played player "may be dropped and his slot clears" and stopped there, which reads as "the slot then stays empty". Measured on the shipped verb: that is true only under `first_game_of_week` (112's whole-week lock refuses the re-seat). Under `per_player_kickoff` — the DEFAULT `lineup_lock` — the emptied slot IS re-seatable by the very next `set_lineup` with a player whose own kickoff is ahead, because the lineup lock has nothing to hold once the stored occupant is unrostered: the team loses the played player's points AND gets a do-over on the slot after seeing his result. Both compositions are now pinned (pgTAP 061 §H1 `first_game_of_week` / §H3 `per_player_kickoff`). **Nothing is cut and no clause changes** — whether the player-level lock should bind drops regardless of the toggle is a product call for Chris, filed with `first_game_of_week`'s fate as PROGRESS **F230**. Amendment sites: §13.1 (the residual sentence + the bullet's version prefix, which claimed "no rule change" over a bullet carrying two v2.16.18 rulings that do — R764). PROGRESS D309(3)/(10), F230, pgTAP 061.

- **v2.16.12 (2026-09-02):** **PATCH — Q30 + Q31 fold-in (the Q10-application precedent: both ruled by Chris 2026-09-02 in-session via the orchestrator, the C34/C41 shape, and applied on the halt PR #248 that filed them — PROGRESS §3 Q30/Q31, blocker B7 cleared, D305, F221).** **Q30 → option (d), divisions CUT from v1.** The L.D1.2 Builder measured that the COUNT of divisions exists everywhere and the MEMBERSHIP nowhere (no DDL column, 0 columns on the live stack, no RPC/route/UI assigns one). Ruling, verbatim: *divisions are pinned at 1 for v1 by spec erratum: the `divisions` setting stays in the catalog and its select shows one option (1); the §7.3.7 tiebreaker-chain entry (5) "Division record (if divisions)" stays inert; §11.7's "Divisions (if 2)" generation clause and the division arms of D289 / D297 / D299 are struck for M4; §16's "division tags" on the schedule view and standings are struck for v1. The engine (L.D1.2) is built WITHOUT the divisions arm. No `teams.division` column, no settings map. Revisit with post-launch feedback.* Return path = PROGRESS **F221** (Q30's option (b): `teams.division` + a seeded auto-split at `in_season` entry). **Q31 → option (b) as offered, riders included: a mid-season floor of 4 regular-season weeks + the shrink chain.** Q29 (ruled 2026-09-01) named no floor; under the catalog's 12 the default league is refused at its LAST PICK from NFL week 5 — the cohort's realistic drafts all land in the band. Ruling: regular season shrinks first, down to **4**; then playoff rounds drop (`playoff_teams` 6 → 4 → 2 → 0); refuse only when floor + 0 playoffs cannot fit (NFL week 16+). Riders: **(1)** `draft_start` pre-flights the fit loudly; completion shrinks silently; completion refuses only on a boundary crossed mid-draft. **(2)** The datum fallback `COALESCE(nfl_weeks.first_kickoff_at, min(nfl_games.kickoff_at), nfl_weeks.starts_at)`, each arm pinned — conservative, never a week under way. **(3)** The typed columns hold the EFFECTIVE plan; §7.3.1's R column becomes *"12–15 at creation; a mid-season league's effective length may be engine-shrunk to ≥ 4"*, with the Zod `min(12)` and the 060/105 SQL range checks relaxed for engine-written rows (L.D1.2 carries both); `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1` continuity kept in league-week terms; NFL week = first week + league week − 1; `league_weeks` rows materialize the plan. **Also folded here for the first time: Q29's mapping itself** (it had no erratum — its trail was tasks-M4 C50/D288): a mid-season league starts at the next NFL week whose first kickoff is still ahead at `in_season` entry. **One derivation said out loud (PROGRESS D305(5)):** for non-default `playoff_teams` a round drop steps to the largest catalog value with one fewer round (12/10 → 8 → 4 → 2 → 0; 8 → 4 → 2 → 0) — the ruling's mechanism applied, not a new rule; 058 pins every step. **Amendment sites:** §7.3.1 `divisions` row (pinned at 1) + `regular_season_weeks` R column + `playoff_start_week` note · §7.3.7 entry (5) annotated inert · §7.3.8 the ≤ 18 bullet's league-week scope + the schedule-invariants bullet's division clause struck · §11.4 the generation-trigger line · §11.5 the Divisions bullet struck · §11.7 the Divisions clause struck + NEW **Mid-season entry** block (the normative home) · §16.2 `schedule-view`/`standings-table` lines, §16.5.2 Schedule & Remix row, §16.5.3 Divisions variant struck · Appendix C prompt 2 · this changelog. tasks-M4 amended at the same sites (lane line, D289/D297/D299, the §5 sketch, L.D1.2 items 2/4/5 with the 058 pins enumerated, L.D1.7, L.D5.2/L.D5.3, C50, §11). No DB change in this PR; L.D1.2 resumes on a fresh branch.

This entry authorized NO build while it awaited markup (the #228/#161/#152 approval-gate precedent — the gate has now passed on PR #239); build waits on the F203 SC.4 cut, takeable at #239's merge.** *(Numbering: at draft time main's header read 2.16.9 with v2.16.10 held by the then-open SC.2 PR #237 — R312 first-filed-keeps; **#237 MERGED mid-draft, 2026-09-01**, and this branch merged it in, so v2.16.11 is simply the next free PATCH above the merged head — no collision remains.)* Chris ruled 2026-08-31/09-01, verbatim (LAW for this draft): *"actually can we have Scout PPR and Scout Standard, so effectively two templates? for PPR our default is .2 pts per reception. does that change anything order wise?"* **The pair:** **Scout Standard** is the SHIPPED 43-value template row (migration 106, `receptions: 0` — byte-for-byte what SC.1 seeded and the #228 markup approved); **Scout PPR** is the identical body with exactly one value changed, **`receptions: 0.2`** — the same 43 keys, one value apart (0.2 is 2dp, guardrail 5 holds; coefficient census 261 → **304**). **The name is a marked assumption, never a silent rename:** the shipped row is named "Scout Scoring" and the name is the NATURAL KEY — SC.3's resolver resolves the system default by it, SC.1's seed and pins carry it (measured: 23 files / 82 occurrences). The draft took the incumbent convention (ESPN Standard / ESPN Full PPR) as a marked assumption — **RULED at markup: "Scout Scoring" → "Scout Standard"** (the fold below) — via a NEW migration's UPDATE by natural key, **never an edit to 106**; B.5.1 enumerates the six touch surfaces (the seeded name · `DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_NAME` · the display-order/one-liner keys · pgTAP 054's 17 literals · 053 §B2's string · the parity suite's name key) and states the alternative with its cost (keep "Scout Scoring" = an asymmetric pair that reads family-name-plus-variant). **Preselection law:** the SYSTEM default stays **Scout Standard** (no-PPR is the FieldScout identity of the original ruling); under the wizard's style filter **each family preselects its own Scout** (No-PPR → Scout Standard, PPR → Scout PPR; the unfiltered mock launcher keeps the system default). routine now); the UI order becomes Scout-variant-first within each style family (table order: Scout Standard, Scout PPR, then the six incumbents unchanged — the table IS the picker order); the wizard's opening side is the only residual choice and it is his **(RULED at markup — the fold below)**. **Scout PPR's card copy was drafted for his edit** in the approved copy's voice — **APPROVED as drafted at markup, the fold below** — (0.2 rewards the catch without letting volume outscore production — contrast full PPR's volume point). **Fixed in passing:** the APPROVED Scout Standard copy's heading still carried its DRAFT-COPY mark (spec:2386) — D278(8)(c)'s blanket approval covered the copy but the #228 fold flipped every B.5 mark except that heading's; - **v2.16.10 (2026-08-31):** **PATCH — arithmetic erratum from SC.2 (the fold-back rule; Builder-carriable on the v2.16.8/D276 retraction precedent — it recomputes an illustrative aside a landed default change falsified, and rules nothing new).** §8.6.1's pacing note printed the pre-Scout default-roster arithmetic — *"15 draftable slots ⇒ 180 nominations … 180 × 20s = 60 minutes"* — which v2.16.9's own §7.3.2 amendment (wr 2 → 3) made false the moment SC.2 landed the default in code (migration 107): the default roster now derives **16 draftable slots ⇒ 192 nominations ⇒ 64 minutes** of opening windows. - **v2.16.9 (2026-08-31):** **PATCH — ~~DRAFT, PENDING CHRIS'S MARKUP~~ [MARKED UP by Chris the same day — his three rulings are folded at the end of this entry]: "Scout Scoring" — FieldScout's own template, the system default for new leagues, and the default roster (NEW Appendix B.5). This entry is NOT Builder-carriable and authorized NO build while it awaited markup (the #161/#152 approval-gate precedent — the gate has now passed; build waits on the F186 SC cut).** Chris described the product intent 2026-08-26 — users entering the scoring flow get **FieldScout defaults**, or pick an incumbent template, and *"we will have some explanation as to why it's better"* (today the only choice is which competitor to imitate) — and ruled the values verbatim the same day: *"Here are the main differences with FieldScout. No PPR, starts 1 QB, 2 RB, 3 WR, 1 WR/RB/TE Flex, 1 TE, 1 DST, 1 K. All TDs are 6 points. All field goals are 3 points. Yards are 10 yards to 1pt for receiving and rushing. 20 yards to 1pt for passing and return yards. Decimal scoring on. -2 for all turnovers. 6 bench spots, 1 IR spot."* Those values are LAW for this draft; **everything he did not state is marked `[ASSUMPTION — Chris to confirm]`** in B.5, each with its basis — unanimity-inherit where the six shipped template bodies agree (XP 1, 2-pt conversions 2, the D/ST event table), majority-with-flag where they disagree (the shared single-model PA family, 4 of 6 rows — the ESPN split model is the minority; `pat_missed` omitted), and **one deliberate anti-majority pick flagged loudest** (`fg_missed` drafted 0 on the flat-FG philosophy where four rows pay −1). The named departures: **every TD 6 — passing TDs included, where ESPN/Yahoo standard pay 4 (the headline)**; **all FGs flat 3** where all six pay 3/4/5 by distance; **receptions 0 as a stated difference**; **passing yards 0.05/yd** vs the industry 0.04. **Two surfaces, deliberately separate:** the SCORING template (seventh `scoring_systems` template row + the system default — a **preselection, never a silent write**: measured, neither the league wizard nor the mock launcher preselects anything today, both init `scoringSystemId: null`) and the ROSTER defaults (1 QB / 2 RB / **3 WR** / 1 TE / 1 FLEX W-R-T / 1 K / 1 D/ST · bench 6 · 1 IR), which are `roster_settings` defaults — **the format-2 envelope has no roster fields and must not grow any**; measured delta from the shipped default is exactly **WR 2 → 3**. **`return_yards` 0.05 is RULED and stays GATED** (Q13/D173/**F71** — no registry key exists; the B.5 table carries the value, the seed omits the key until the data task, F71 annotated with the Scout step, and nothing here un-gates anything). **Name history cited, not hidden:** *"Scout Scoring"* was invented once and WITHDRAWN with no values behind it (PROGRESS D229(2), 2026-08-21); this entry re-mints it with the ruling behind it, and the name is Chris's to change at markup. **Amendment sites:** §7.3.3 template table + NEW system-default bullet · §7.3.2 preset table (wr) + roster note · §8.8 v2.16 block (six → seven, parenthetical) · §16.2 picker line · Appendix B preamble + NEW B.5 · this changelog. **Build NOT authorized:** after markup, tasks **SC.1–SC.3** (B.5's build hook) build it against the **measured blast radius in PROGRESS D278(4)** — every "exactly six templates" pin with its count and the command that measured it; a 7th template is a NEW migration (next free number per D161/D166; `HELD-FROM-PRODUCTION.txt` same PR), never an edit to 058. PROGRESS **D278**; ledger **F186**. **MARKED UP by Chris 2026-08-31 — three rulings, folded into the same PR (#228), satisfying the approval-gate sentence above (build still waits on the F186 SC cut): (1) `fg_missed` = −1** — he took the incumbent majority, **overriding the draft's flagged anti-majority 0** (the flag did its job); `pat_missed` deliberately does NOT move with it — the ruling was FG-specific and `pat_missed 0` is blanket-approved (stated at both rows so nobody infers a pair). **(2) The ESPN-style SPLIT D/ST model** — the 2-of-6 minority pattern, **overriding the draft's 4-of-6 majority single-table pick**: the 8-key ESPN PA family + the 9-key YA table on the shipped ESPN templates' own Q9-verified values (`ESPN_DEF_PA`/`ESPN_DEF_YA` read from `templates.ts`, byte-equal in 058 — never memory), `dst_model = 'split'`; the coefficient count recounts **32 → 43**; both cut lists are §23.5-PUBLISHED families and all 17 tier names sit inside the published-20 allowlist, so **no Q26/F137 tension is created**; 053 §B2's family detection resolves Scout to the ESPN 8-cut list exactly as it does the two ESPN rows. **(3) Everything else APPROVED AS DRAFTED** — the unanimity inherits (XP 1, 2-pt 2, D/ST event values), "all TDs 6" covering D/ST TDs, lost-fumbles-only −2, the IR reading, **the name "Scout Scoring"**, and the user-facing copy (checked: the copy never claimed one-table D/ST simplicity — it mentions D/ST nowhere — so the split ruling forces no reword). Every B.5 mark flipped to **RULED (Chris, 2026-08-31)** or **APPROVED as drafted (Chris, 2026-08-31)** with the draft's basis kept as the trail; the two overrides keep the draft's original pick in their rows — that asymmetry is the record of the gate working.
- **v2.16.8 (2026-08-31):** **PATCH — E81's CLOSED-ROOM half was factually wrong and is corrected (the fold-back rule; Builder-carriable: it retracts a measurement error in a sentence v2.16.7 minted eight days ago and rules nothing new).** E81 said *"once the room closes, the client's default `gcTime` (5 minutes) evicts the entry and a later mount refetches regardless"* — an unconditional claim that a reopened room is fresh. **Measured against the installed `@tanstack/query-core` 5.96.1** with a virtual clock and the browser branch forced (`environmentManager.setIsServer(() => false)`): a mount, a reconnect and a focus event are gated **alike** through `shouldFetchOn → isStale → query.isStaleByTime(staleTime)`, and `shouldFetchOnMount` short-circuits to an unconditional load **only while `query.state.data === undefined`** — i.e. only once the entry has actually been evicted. **A room closed and reopened inside 5 minutes, over data younger than its `staleTime`, does not refetch** (measured: fetches 1 → 1, entry still present; evicted first, 1 → 2). So the sentence now says **no timer guarantees a bound in EITHER direction**, and keeps one nuance honest: repeated close/reopen cycles *do* eventually refresh once the data crosses `staleTime` (5 fetches over 15 four-minute cycles, measured), which is a tendency and not a bound. **Nothing about the behaviour E81 requires changes** — invalidation was already the mechanism; what changes is that the spec no longer offers a timer as a fallback that does not exist. PROGRESS **D276**; ledger **F173**.
- **v2.16.7 (2026-08-26):** **PATCH — §15.1 prints the two custom-scoring routes (the fold-back rule; Builder-carriable on the C40/v2.8.17 route-erratum precedent — it prints routes for RPCs §12.25 already ruled server-authoritative, and rules nothing new).** §7.3.3.1 and §12.25 have specified the editor's fork-and-save since v2.11 and left the wire surface to *"the follow-up breakdown's"* call; §15 carried no scoring route (`grep -n 'scoring/fork\|scoring/rules'` → no hits before this entry). SE.6 builds them, so SE.6 folds them: **`POST /api/leagues/[id]/scoring/fork`** → `scoring_fork_template` and **`PUT /api/leagues/[id]/scoring/rules`** → `scoring_update_rules` (migration 105), both commissioner-only with the league in `setup`/`scheduled`. **PUT, not PATCH**, because the wire body is the WHOLE document and §7.3.3.1's normal form is a property of the whole — a partial merge would make the derived All-Positions switch state depend on what the client happened to send. **Reads still need no route** (§12.25's member SELECT policy; the snapshot post-draft), which is why the block says so out loud. **New §19.2 E81** records the one behaviour the routes owe beyond calling their RPCs: **every** writer of the league's scoring document **invalidates the league's scoring query** — the two routes here and the settings PATCH that re-picks a template. Before SE.6 nothing in `src/` invalidated it, so a successful edit left the editing client's own open room serving pre-edit scoring silently, with no timer bounding it (the app sets `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`, so a mounted room has no automatic refetch trigger at all). PROGRESS **D275**.
- **v2.16.6 (2026-08-25):** **PATCH — MS.8 build errata (the fold-back rule; Builder-carriable: both are edge-case semantics of a control v2.15 already ruled, and rule nothing new).** Two decisions the slot-at-launch build (migration 102) had to make where v2.15/E77 were silent, folded into **E77**: **(a) drawn-order interaction** — a launch-time slot against a league whose order is already drawn (a stored lobby order, or manual/custom) **refuses by name** rather than being silently ignored or overriding the order; §8.8 fidelity governs (the mock inherits a drawn order), and the slotless launch stays the sanctioned path. The launcher UI disables the picker with the reason where the drawn order is client-visible (manual/custom settings order); the RPC refusal is the authority for the case it cannot see (a lobby-randomized order on the real draft row). **(b) the auction meaning** — the slot is the **nomination position too**: "slot 3" means you nominate third; `same_as_draft_order` makes the two orders coincide by construction, and an independently-random nomination order pins the human at the same slot over its own derived seed. Default random reproduces the pre-102 shuffle byte-for-byte (the backward-compat pin, pgTAP 050). PROGRESS **D261**.
- **v2.16.5 (2026-08-25):** **PATCH — schema erratum from AP.6/migration 099 (the fold-back rule; Builder-carriable per the v2.10.2/D102 spec-absent-schema precedent — it prints a mechanism E69 already ruled, and rules nothing new).** E69 (v2.13) made `draft_adjust_budget` idempotent via E2's select-then-insert replay returning the **original** result, but the spec recorded no store for the replay to select from — `drafts.budget_adjustments` is one cumulative number per team (D127) and cannot answer "what did THIS action return". New **§12.26 `draft_budget_adjustments`**: one row per landed edit carrying the original return payload verbatim, deduped by a partial unique in §12.5's C39 pattern (`uniq_draft_budget_adjust_action … WHERE action_id IS NOT NULL` — unstamped rows coexist), RLS SELECT for league members, no write policy (appended only by the DEFINER verb, §8.1). The signature gains `p_action_id UUID DEFAULT NULL` (last, defaulted — every existing call text binds to NULL and composes exactly as before); the route requires `action_id` wire-side and `useAdjustBudget` mints one per submit (the D68/D114(4) force-pick contract). No budget arithmetic reads the new table; an `action_id` stays an idempotency key, not an audit record (`commissioner_actions` is M6). Shipped in migration 099 with pgTAP 047 (PROGRESS D256; discharges **F82**).
- **v2.16.4 (2026-08-25):** **PATCH — §8.9's *"My Lists"* panel gets its standalone answer, the half v2.16.3 named and left open (task MP.6c; PROGRESS D247(9), discharging **F122**; folded by the Builder per the fold-back rule).** A standalone practice room offers **the caller's own lists, Big Board first, and nothing else** — v2.16.3's OWNERSHIP predicate, applied to the SURFACE so it cannot offer what the verb refuses (D110(1)). The three attachment controls and the best-available helper do not render, because `league_lists` rows — including `is_primary_board` — cannot exist without a league; that is an absence with a cause, not a gap to fill with a default. **No behaviour on any real league, no DDL, no policy** (the client reads the `lists` query the panel already ran). **The league arm is untouched and its pins pass unmoved.** Filed alongside, not decided here: **F123** — `MockRow`'s now-live *Rejoin* is an entry into the room that does not take DR.6's desktop split, dispositioned in place for MP.10.
- **v2.16.3 (2026-08-24):** **PATCH — §8.9's load-into-queue gets its standalone arm, because §8.9 scopes lists by a league tag and a standalone practice draft has no league (task MP.6b; PROGRESS D245; folded by the Builder per the fold-back rule).** MP.6b gives the room's seven verbs a league-free door, and `queue-from-list` is the one of them that reads a SECOND table with a league-shaped scope (`league_lists`). The standalone arm scopes by **ownership** — the caller's own list — which is §8.8 v2.16's *"one ownership predicate, not a membership graph"* applied one table over, and it is **narrower** than the league arm: a public list somebody else owns is readable under `lists` RLS and is still refused (pinned — `standalone-actions-db.test.ts` §A drives exactly that list and expects the refusal). **The league arm is untouched** and its own pins pass unmoved. **No behaviour on any real league, no DDL, no policy, no migration** — MP.6b is a service-and-route change on top of 095, which already carried every RPC's standalone arm. **What this entry does not decide:** which lists a standalone room's *panel* offers (PROGRESS **F122**), and the separate, live-proven engine defect MP.6b measured while driving a standalone auction (**F121** — `draft_nomination_uncontestable` filters `t.league_id = v_league_id`, so on a NULL league every nomination is declared uncontestable and awarded instantly; a standalone practice auction has no bidding at all until it is fixed, and its fix is v2.16.2's own idiom — a `league_id IS NULL` arm over the mock's `drafts.draft_order` seat map, never an `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` widening).
- **v2.16.2 (2026-08-24):** **PATCH — §12's DDL and its three draft-path SELECT policies catch up with the standalone mock v2.16 already ruled into v1 (task MP.3, migration 095; PROGRESS D234/D227, F109(d); folded by the Builder per the fold-back rule).** §8.8 has said since **v2.16** that a mock is standalone and needs no league; §12 went on printing `league_id … NOT NULL` on `drafts`, `draft_picks` and `draft_bids` and `USING (is_league_member(league_id))` on all three SELECT policies. Those listings were **stale, not contradictory** — but they are LAW as printed, and the task that changes a policy owes the erratum. **What changes in the text:** the three `league_id` columns are printed **NULLABLE** (the FK is KEPT in every case — a NULL passes an FK, a wrong id still fails), and the three policies gain the standalone-launcher arm. **The `league_id IS NULL` conjunct inside each arm is load-bearing and is printed with the reason**: MP.1 probed the arm without it and an **EX-MEMBER kept read on a LEAGUE-attached mock they had launched before leaving the league** — strictly more permissive on a row that HAS a league, which is what the never-weaken rule forbids (PROGRESS D234(7) arm C). With the conjunct, the predicate reduces to the shipped one term for term on every league-owning row, so the arms are **provably inert** for real leagues rather than merely believed to be. Now a permanent test (pgTAP 043 §B) instead of a rolled-back probe. **`league_chat.league_id` is nullable too** — the engine's pause/resume system posts are written for a standalone mock with a NULL `league_id` rather than skipped, because those posts are what the room reads back — but that table's DDL is not printed in this spec (it predates it, §12's header note), so there is no listing here to correct. **Nothing else changes:** no behaviour on any real league or `is_mock = FALSE` draft, no new table, no new column, no index. §8.8's zero-side-effects rule is unweakened and is measured on the new paths (043 §F/§G).
- **v2.16.1 (2026-08-24):** **PATCH — the roster shape is now snapshotted, and real drafts keep the live read (task MP.2, migration 094; PROGRESS D236; folded by the Builder per the fold-back rule).** §8.8's v2.16 Launch erratum stated obligation **(b)** in the present tense as an open defect (*"It is not today"*); it is closed, and a LAW sentence that describes a fixed defect as live is stale LAW. `create_mock_draft` writes the league's whole `roster_settings` object into `drafts.config->'roster'` at launch, every pre-094 mock was backfilled from its own league, and `draft_autopick_resolve` / `draft_mock_cpu_need` read the snapshot on a mock's path — `draft_mock_cpu_need` no longer names `public.leagues` at all. **The one thing this entry ADDS to the LAW rather than records: a REAL draft keeps the LIVE league read, on purpose.** §7.3.3's scoring-snapshot doctrine and D95 both point at snapshot-at-start as the house position, but a real draft's settings edits go through §8.7/D141's pause-first commissioner controls, which is a different argument with a different blast radius; MP.2 had no mandate to settle it and deliberately did not. Recorded here so the next lane inherits the decision instead of re-deciding it silently, **and carried as PROGRESS ledger row F111** — an inherited decision with no ledger row is how a deferral becomes an accident (R493). **Nothing else changes:** no policy, no DDL, no signature, and an `is_mock = FALSE` draft's autopick behaviour is byte-identical to v2.13's (pinned, pgTAP 042 §D).
- **v2.16 (2026-08-21):** **A MOCK DRAFT IS FOR PRACTICING, AND IT IS A STANDALONE THING — two premises corrected, and the second one deleted more than the first (Chris, in-session 2026-08-21; folded by the Architect per the fold-back rule — PR open for approval).** **MINOR bump, not a patch:** this entry reverses a v1-scope statement the LAW made and retires an acceptance criterion. **Companion breakdown `docs/specs/tasks-MP-mock-practice.md` (prefix `MP.`, eleven tasks); PROGRESS D225–D233, F102–F108, Q24, E78–E80.** **(1) THE PREMISE (§8.8's new v2.16 block and its amended v1-scope bullet).** *"a user may run a mock draft without even being 'in a league'."* **Practice is the purpose; a league is optional context.** The **standalone** mock is now **v1** and the **league-attached** one is **DEFERRED, not cancelled** — Chris rates it highly (*"a really awesome feature that current platforms fail to do effectively"*) and deferred it on sequencing: *"we can get back to the league based mocks after we actually have demand for the league product itself."* **(2) THE INHERITED THREAT MODEL DOES NOT APPLY, AND THIS IS THE ENTRY'S LOAD-BEARING RULE.** The first cut of the breakdown answered the corrected premise by designing a hidden per-user "practice container" — a `leagues` row nobody sees — so a league-less mock could still satisfy league-shaped RLS, league-shaped FKs and this section's isolation rule. Chris: *"I doubt they are doing any of this inivisible league blah blah blah security container stuff."* **He is right, and the reason generalises: Mock Draft Mode was built INSIDE the leagues system and inherited its whole protective apparatus — RLS by membership, zero-side-effects isolation, commissioner authority, audit immutability — all of which exists because a real draft has twelve humans with money at stake. A mock has one human and eleven bots: no adversary, nothing to protect.** The complexity was **inherited, not required**, and nobody re-examined it when the purpose changed. **Two obligations survive and neither is about adversaries:** a user must not be able to break their own practice irrecoverably (a live mock's bot seats are not deletable — deleting one wedges the draft at that seat's turn), and one user's practice is not another's to read or drive — **one ownership predicate, not a membership graph.** **§8.8's zero-side-effects rule is NOT weakened**: it keeps full force for a league-attached mock, and for a standalone one it becomes *nothing leaks into the league product*, established by a whole-schema measurement rather than asserted. **(3) SETTINGS COME FROM THE SIX BASE SCORING TEMPLATES** (§7.3.3 / App B, already shipped) **plus this spec's own draft defaults, and the launcher changes them at launch** — *"the defaults are merely a suggestion."* **Design obligation on the shipping half:** the launch path takes a settings **object** and does not care where it came from, so league inheritance later is a **new source, not a rewrite**. **(4) NO MOCK REFUSES TO START FOR WANT OF HUMANS.** D103(1)'s full-seat-map rule produced a live refusal in real use — *"practice draft failed, it says i need 11 more people"* — and it was the architecture answering correctly to the wrong question. It was a statement about borrowing a league's franchises and **has no subject for a standalone mock**, which mints its own opponents. A related defect is named in the Launch bullet: the roster **shape** is read LIVE from the league by `draft_autopick_resolve` and `draft_mock_cpu_need`, which already breaks D95's *"a mock never re-hydrates"*. **(5) THE MOCK DRAFT REPORT, ON THE USER RATHER THAN THE LEAGUE.** A flat table with **two column sets, never one table with blanks** (auction: price + nomination #; snake: round + pick #), reachable from a **Mock Drafts** entry under **More…**; the league recap keeps real drafts unchanged and mock links **redirect** rather than break. Whether that user-scoped surface is private app chrome or a public profile tab is **Q24** (recommendation: private). **(6) MOCKS ARE INDEPENDENT OF LEAGUE WORK** (ruled). Their own release gate; **turning leagues off must not turn practice off**; and **independence is a claim about ROUTES as well as components** — the practice room, list and report carry a route family of their own, keyed on the mock's id. Still a release statement and not code isolation: the shared draft engine stays shared, and the gate never touches a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC. **(7) THE CLOCKS, RULED DIRECTLY AND IDENTICAL FOR MOCKS AND REAL LEAGUES:** nomination **30s**, bid **20s**, anti-snipe **10s** — *"Use the same default."* All three are already the shipped values, so **zero lines change**. **§8.8's fidelity sentence stands unamended and goes dormant** with the deferred half. **PROGRESS Q23 LAPSES** — it asked whether a mock-specific bid clock would override a league's chosen one, and there is no longer a mock-specific default or a league to inherit from; **AP.4 closes with no code and B5 clears.** **(8) THE 45-MINUTE ACCEPTANCE BAR IS DE-TARGETED** (§8.8's acceptance-bar erratum): *"just an example not a target … with all the other fixes we've put in I think we're good."* **PROGRESS F85 stops being a gate**; F92 survives as a pacing cost worth fixing rather than a term in a bar. **What this entry does NOT weaken:** §8.8's zero-side-effects rule, its fidelity sentence, its v2.15 launcher-is-the-commissioner block, and §8.7 in any particular. §19.2 gains **E78–E80**.
- **v2.15 (2026-08-20):** **THE MOCK IS YOUR SANDBOX — the launcher holds the commissioner's tools (Chris, in-session 2026-08-20; folded by the Architect per the fold-back rule — PR open for approval).** **MINOR bump, not a patch:** this entry amends a rule the LAW stated and changes who may act on a draft. **Companion breakdown `docs/specs/tasks-MS-mock-sandbox.md` (prefix `MS.`, six tasks); nothing is built until Chris approves it.** **What changes.** §8.8 gains a new block: **the launcher is the commissioner of their own mock**, keyed on `config.mock.launched_by` and nothing else — *"in a mock my assumption was that the solo person is the commish so the user running the mock should be able to make it as fast as possible IMO and pause it if they need to do something."* This **amends** the v2.8.16 erratum's clause that *every* §8.7 control refuses mocks. That erratum was right about **authority** (a control on somebody else's practice is interference) and it caught a real bug (an unguarded `draft_reset` was a live-proven `leagues` write); what it missed is that **in a mock the launcher and the commissioner are the same person**, so there is nobody to interfere with. §8.7's pause-first bullet gains **one carve-out, mocks only**: the **clock edit is not pause-first on a mock**. Pause-first exists to stop a clock moving under eleven other people mid-bid; solo it protects nobody and costs a pause every time the launcher tries a different number. **What emphatically does NOT change.** **(a) Every real draft, in every respect** — D141 + the F57 ALIGN ruling (v2.12.5) stand exactly as ruled at every draft type, every refusal message stays byte-identical, and the carve-out is `is_mock`, never `draft_type`. **(b) D103(2)'s no-other-driver doctrine** — a league commissioner who did not launch the mock still gets nothing; authority MOVES to the launcher, it is not shared. **(c) §8.8's zero-side-effects rule**, which is now the explicit **gate on every control**: a mock writes nothing to the real league — no `league_rosters`, no `transactions`, no status transitions, no notifications, no `leagues`/`league_members`/`teams` write, and no broadcast on the `league:<id>` topic. **(d) The E15 rule** — a new clock value governs the NEXT clock and never rewrites a running one; both `extend_current` arms stay retired. **Which controls open is decided by MEASUREMENT, per control** (whole-schema delta plus a **whole-row composite** over the real league — a row count cannot see an in-place UPDATE, and several of these controls perform one). **PARITY IS THE GOVERNING RULE, ruled after the breakdown's first review:** *"if real leagues can a mock league should as well so everyone can simulate their actual draft position or test different ones if they have a choice — for example, in my league last place gets to choose their draft position the next year."* A mock gets whatever a real league gets, **not whatever is safe to give it**, so a control that fails the isolation rule is **fixed where fixing it is possible** and refused only where it writes the real league by construction. Concretely the launcher can **set their mock's draft (and nomination) order** and **choose their starting slot at launch** — 1..N, default random, requiring no real order to exist (*"there is no real draft order, most drafts will set the order right before"*). **Three refusals are known at fold time, not one:** `draft_reset` (the original breach — a mock-safe variant is task MS.4's decision and, if built, must return the mock to `live` at pick 1, since `draft_start_internal` excludes mocks and a `scheduled` mock could never restart); the **autopick toggle** (`set_team_autodraft` writes the real `league_members.is_autodraft` by construction and a mock never reads the flag); and **seat reassignment** (the league's real invite/assignment surface). **A FOURTH control failed the rule and is FIXED, not refused: "Edit draft order" is MIS-TARGETED rather than blocked** — its request carries no draft id, so the service picks the league's real active draft and the RPC's mock guard never runs. It is corrected at the **service layer** so the mock room targets its own draft; refusing it was the wrong remedy and would have cost the user the ability to practise from their real slot. **Severity, because it is not a row count:** a draft order is negotiated property (*"two people text messaging agree to swap picks"*), so a silent reversal destroys an off-platform agreement while posting a system line that makes it look deliberate. **The general rule this establishes: an RPC-level measurement cannot see a mis-target** — a control is proven mock-safe only when driven through the surface a user reaches it by, with a real draft alive in the same league. *(The commissioner **swap** Chris named next — today's editor is a move-and-shift that displaces bystanders — is real-league scope, PROGRESS **F101**, and acquires no trade system.)* Each remaining refusal names **its own** reason — the class-wide sentence *"mock drafts have no commissioner controls"* retires with this entry because it stops being true. **Copy RULED and closed (Chris: "no"):** no teaching label, no tutorial, no onboarding hint. His *"it could be a good way for random people to know what tools a commish has"* is an observed **consequence** of the tools existing, not a feature to build on top; the requirement is that the controls are **findable** where commissioner controls live (§16.4's command bar), never that anything explains them. **Governing note for the area, also his:** *"a commish can pause a draft and change anything or someone can disconnect at any time. TBH in drafts that's known as a 'personal problem'"* — no defensive machinery for humans changing their minds or dropping connection (the engine's own guards are the contract, not "defence"). **Amendment sites:** §8.7's pause-first bullet · §8.8 (the v2.8.16 erratum amended in place + a new v2.15 block incl. the parity rule) · §19.2 **E75/E76/E77**. **§16.2/§16.4 are deliberately untouched** — the copy ruling means the surfacing needs no new spec copy, and PR #180 is concurrently rewriting §16.4. **Numbering:** this entry takes **v2.15**, leaving **v2.14 reserved for the in-flight draft-room-v2 entry on PR #180** (first-filed keeps its number — the R312 principle, the same resolution v2.11/v2.12 took); whichever merges second resolves the version header, the changelog head and §19.2's table tail deliberately. PROGRESS **D216–D223**, **F98–F101**, **Q22**.
- **v2.13.4 (2026-08-20):** **Chris corrects the ORDER of §8.6.9's instant award, plus three build-time errata from AP.1's sibling task (migration 093).** **(1) THE ORDER, ruled:** *"No you've misunderstood. It awards the player immediately and then displays that message for 3 seconds until the next nomination."* v2.13's §8.6.9 and §16.5.4 both printed the message running BEFORE the board painted — *"holds it for 3 seconds before painting the board"*, *"then the board paints the award"* — and that is backwards. The server finds the nomination uncontestable and **awards it immediately, atomically, in that same determination**; the 3-second message then runs **over an already-painted board**, in the gap that already exists before the next nomination, in the same family as the room's other nomination pacing. It is a display/pacing beat and never a decision window, so nothing is deferred, nothing is pending, and nothing on the server waits. E67's row is corrected the same way. **(2) NO WINDOW MEANS NO DEFENCES, and that is a ruling.** *"but a commish can pause a draft and change anything or someone can disconnect at any time. TBH in drafts that's known as a 'personal problem'"* — **pause → change → resume (§8.7) is the sanctioned mechanism** for changing a live draft and a mid-auction disconnect is what §8.6.2's system nomination is for, so §8.6.9 owes no pending state, no re-check-at-expiry, no cancellation and no reconciliation path. Recorded as a bullet in §8.6.9 so their absence reads as the ruling it is rather than as an oversight the next session "fixes". What still stands, because it defends against two *writers* rather than a change of mind: the draft-row lock, §8.1/E2 idempotency, `uniq_draft_player_live`. **(3) §12.3's `current_nomination` gains an additive, WIRE-ONLY `uncontested: true` key** (D199(4), anticipated by the decision and folded here now that it ships): the uncontestable path's phase flip carries it and 088/D134 already broadcasts `current_nomination` whole, so the room learns of the instant award with no new column, no new event name and no new topic. Both statements commit together, so the key **never comes to rest** — any SELECT already reads NULL — which is what makes §16.5.4's "a reconnect cannot lose the award", "a late joiner sees the completed award" and "never show it twice" structural rather than a matter of client discipline. The same flip writes `current_deadline` **NULL**, making E67's *"no bid clock is ever opened"* true on the wire and not only in the row. **(4) THERE ARE THREE NOMINATION PATHS, not two.** §8.6.9 is written about nominations with no qualifier on who opened one, so it applies to `draft_nominate`, `draft_system_nominate_internal` **and `draft_force_pick`'s auction arm** — the commissioner force-nomination (§8.7/R301). AP.2's breakdown named the first two; the third was found at build time, exactly as AP.1 found the same function missing from its own read-site table (PROGRESS D214(1)). No behaviour in this entry is new scope: (1), (2) and (4) are Chris's rule stated correctly, and (3) is the mechanism D199 already chose.
- **v2.13.3 (2026-08-20):** **Withdrawal of v2.13.2's erratum 2, which was wrong in the direction that matters (AP.1 review, R456).** v2.13.2 recorded §7.3.8's auction solvency bullet as *"vacuous for schema-valid settings in both columns"* and rested that on `roster_size` topping out at 46. **The premise is false and is withdrawn.** The 20-slot starting-lineup cap is a **validation rule of §7.3.8 itself**, not a schema bound: the settings schema bounds each starting slot's `count` at 10 and **does not bound the array length at all**, so a 30-slot lineup parses clean, derives a **307-player roster**, and makes a $50 budget fail this bullet **per-field** — observed at that layer (`leagueSettingsSchema.parse` OK; `validateLeagueSettings` returns `roster_settings.starting_slots` AND `draft.auction_budget`), not inferred from the printed text. **What is true, and is what the bullet now says:** at reserve 1 the arm is REDUNDANT WITH ITS SIBLING — nothing violates solvency without also violating the roster-size bullet — so it never fires alone. **Redundancy between two arms of one validator is not vacuity**, and the difference is load-bearing, because a guard recorded as vacuous is a guard the next session deletes: on the strength of the false premise this PR had already downgraded the API-surface solvency assertion to a Zod-range check, and measurement showed that with the arm made dead code the downgraded test stayed GREEN while the restored one goes RED. Both are now pinned. No behaviour changes; what changes is that the spec stops licensing a deletion.
- **v2.13.2 (2026-08-20):** **Two errata from AP.1 (migration 092), the fold-back rule — both about text v2.13 itself left behind.** **(1) §8.6.2's v2.12.1 erratum still named `auction_min_bid`**, the field v2.13 retired one section earlier, as the opening bid of a system nomination. It now names **the nomination floor** — the derived number of §7.3.8's `auction_zero_dollar_nominations` row — which is what the engine has always written and what migration 092 reads through its one authority. No behaviour changes; the sentence stops pointing at a field that no longer exists. **(2) §7.3.8's auction solvency bullet is now redundant with its sibling roster-size bullet in both toggle columns**, and says so. *(The wording of this half as originally published — "vacuous for schema-valid settings", resting on a 46-player roster ceiling — was WRONG and is withdrawn by **v2.13.3**; the ceiling is a validation rule, not a schema bound. Kept here rather than silently rewritten, so the correction is visible.)* v2.13 already recorded that the ON column satisfies it trivially; what was not recorded is that at reserve 1 the OFF column can only be violated by a roster the sibling bullet already refuses. The rule is **not** removed (never-weaken, tasks-M3 §4 rule 7) — it is defence in depth at the API surface, it is still exercised directly one dollar either side, and the engine's own start gate is a different and smaller count (D91 draftable slots, no IR) that CAN still refuse. Recorded so the next session reads a live invariant as live rather than as dead code to tidy away.
- **v2.13.1 (2026-08-20):** **Erratum from AP.3 (migration 091), the fold-back rule — §8.8's CPU-speed-toggle bullet no longer describes the shipped bots.** The v2.13 ruling that a CPU raise is *provoked by the bid it answers, never by the sweep* makes a raise **untimed**, so the `realistic`/`fast` toggle — printed as governing "bot think-time" — now governs the auction **nomination** think and ARM 2.5's snake picks only. The bullet says so, and names the one texture that is genuinely lost: a CPU raise landing inside the final anti-snipe seconds. Nothing is re-ruled; this is the printed catalog catching up with behaviour Chris ordered. (The remaining sweep latency on the CPU *nomination* is PROGRESS **F92**, filed with its measured cost.)
- **v2.13 (2026-08-20):** **Auction pacing, $0 nominations, and the manual nomination order — Chris's 2026-08-20 rulings, folded (six of them, after he drove the finished M3 auction UI). MINOR bump, not a patch: this entry removes a setting, adds two, changes engine behaviour and adds an acceptance bar — the v2.10 / v2.11 / v2.12 precedent for a ruled product batch, where the `.x` band is reserved for errata.** Build authorized by the companion breakdown `docs/specs/tasks-AP-auction-pacing.md` (Architect, 2026-08-20; prefix `AP.`), which sequences ahead of M3's tail (L.C4.1/L.C5.1/L.C6.1) — Chris's ordering, because those three tasks would otherwise be written against behaviour about to change.
  1. **Bot pacing is a product requirement with a number on it (§8.6.3 pacing note, §8.8).** Chris's targets from real use: a **mock auction under 45 minutes**, a live auction under 90; ours measured ≈ 3.5–4 h for a mock (F76). Three causes, all measured: CPU raises rode the 5s `draft-tick` sweep (5 s of wall-clock per $1); CPUs raised by exactly $1 per pass; and the bid clock is the floor under everything. §8.8 now requires **CPU responses to be provoked by the action that provoked them, in that transaction** — the sweep keeps expiring clocks and is never made faster to solve a mock problem — and requires **jump-bids within value** (*"a bot can bid $35 or $40 immediately … They also bid super fast in $1 increments"*). This supersedes D132's `+$1`-once-per-sweep letter and answers **Q15**; the value model is unchanged.
  2. **Uncontestable nominations award instantly — NEW §8.6.9, E67.** *"I would prefer a 3 second tick with the message. 'No one can bid. Awarding Player Name to the Nominator.'"* Condition: no OTHER active franchise can reach `high_bid + increment` (written that way rather than `> high_bid` on purpose). Applies anywhere in the draft. The award lands **in the nomination's own transaction** — same award path, same solvency re-check, same rotation — and the **3 seconds is the room's presentation beat** (§16.5.4), not a server delay, because a tick-scheduled 3 s would land anywhere in 3–8 s. The copy question (*"the Nominator"* vs the team's name) is **Q18**, deliberately not decided here.
